Purpose
-------
This file is the durable conversation between the brain agent and the worker agent.
The brain owns priorities, architectural decisions, review, and final acceptance.
The worker owns bounded implementation and evidence gathering. Both append dated entries;
do not erase or rewrite earlier entries. Git commits and pushes require a brain decision.

Shared protocol
---------------
1. Before work, read HANDOFF-SETS-RING.md, repository AGENTS.md, README.md, and
   ARCHITECTURE.md, plus the canonical Papers AGENTS.md and HERMES.md named by AGENTS.md.
2. Start every worker entry with WORKER and every brain entry with BRAIN.
3. Record hypotheses before changing behavior; record exact commands and results afterward.
4. Preserve creator data and unrelated changes. Do not commit, push, merge, release, launch,
   terminate, or install anything unless a later BRAIN entry explicitly authorizes it.
5. Tests must exercise behavior, not source text. Check edited files for NUL bytes.
6. A worker may stop and report uncertainty. The brain reviews every material change.

======================================================================
BRAIN OPERATING PROTOCOL v2 — SUPERSEDES THE EARLIER SHARED PROTOCOL
======================================================================

This section controls all work after it. If an earlier instruction conflicts with this
section or a later dated BRAIN entry, follow the latest dated BRAIN entry. Never infer that
silence means approval.

Roles and authority
-------------------
BRAIN:
- Converts creator intent into bounded assignments and acceptance criteria.
- Owns product interpretation, architecture, priority, trade-offs, and final acceptance.
- Reviews evidence and decides whether to continue, revise, commit, push, or stop.

WORKER (the creator's separate long-horizon harness):
- Investigates, reproduces, implements, tests, measures, and reports within the active
  assignment.
- May make small reversible implementation choices that clearly follow existing patterns.
- Must not invent product behavior, broaden scope, tune around an unexplained mechanism,
  or treat passing tests as proof of a human-visible result.
- Must not create or delegate to more agents unless a later BRAIN entry explicitly asks.

Absolute boundaries
-------------------
- Work only in D:\Letters\MatTroiSeConMoc\Papers\Backpack projects\As you Go.
- Do not modify the canonical Papers repository, packaged Papers files, state.json, the
  backup state file, or anything outside this repository.
- Do not commit, amend, rebase, merge, switch branches, push, open a pull request, release,
  install, launch, close, or restart Papers unless a later BRAIN entry explicitly says so.
- Do not discard, overwrite, stage, or "clean up" changes you did not create.
- Do not use destructive Git or filesystem commands. Do not alter test expectations merely
  to make a failure disappear.
- BRAIN-WORKER.txt is append-only from this point onward. Never rewrite prior entries.

Mandatory pickup sequence
-------------------------
Before editing code, read these files completely in this exact order:
1. BRAIN-WORKER.txt (read through the final line; the latest dated BRAIN entry wins).
2. HANDOFF-SETS-RING.md.
3. README.md.
4. AGENTS.md.
5. D:\Letters\MatTroiSeConMoc\PAPERS 3\Papers-3\AGENTS.md.
6. D:\Letters\MatTroiSeConMoc\PAPERS 3\Papers-3\HERMES.md.
7. ARCHITECTURE.md.

Then run read-only preflight checks:
- git status --short --branch
- git log --oneline --decorate -12
- git diff --stat and git diff
- Confirm the active branch is feat/sets-ring.
- Identify all pre-existing changes. At present BRAIN-WORKER.txt is intentionally untracked;
  anything else must be treated as creator/other-harness work until proven otherwise.

Append a WORKER PICKUP entry before editing. It must state:
- The active assignment number and your one-sentence understanding of its outcome.
- Current branch/head and pre-existing working-tree changes.
- Files and commits you expect to inspect.
- Your falsifiable first hypothesis.
- Any contradiction among the instructions or repository evidence.

Long-horizon work loop
----------------------
For each assignment, repeat this loop instead of wandering into adjacent problems:

1. ORIENT
   Restate the exact observable failure and acceptance criteria. Trace the owning module and
   existing tests. Read relevant history before proposing a new mechanism.

2. REPRODUCE
   Build the smallest deterministic behavioral reproduction that fails for the stated
   reason. Confirm it fails on the current code. Record the exact failure. A test that passes
   before the fix is not evidence and must be redesigned.

3. EXPLAIN
   Locate the mechanism. Use focused probes or ablation where causality is uncertain. Record
   what observation would disprove the explanation. Do not change production code yet if the
   cause is still ambiguous.

4. CHANGE
   Make the smallest coherent correction in the owning module. Preserve public contracts and
   existing interaction rules. Comments explain why. Do not perform opportunistic refactors.

5. PROVE
   Run the new regression test, relevant focused suites, then npm test. Mutation-check the
   test: temporarily neutralize or reverse the corrected rule, verify the new test fails for
   the intended reason, restore the correction, and verify it passes. Confirm the mutation
   actually changed the loaded file.

6. MEASURE
   When performance or physics is involved, use a repeatable harness with the constants and
   gesture shape shipped by the app. Report setup, warm-up, repetitions, distribution or at
   least median/range, and before/after numbers. Do not present a single timing as a result.

7. AUDIT
   Review git diff, list every changed file, inspect for accidental scope growth, and scan
   edited text files for NUL bytes. Re-run git status. Do not include state.json changes.

8. REPORT
   Append a structured WORKER CHECKPOINT or WORKER COMPLETION entry using the templates below.
   Continue autonomously between checkpoints unless a stop condition applies.

Checkpoint cadence
------------------
Append a WORKER CHECKPOINT at each of these moments:
- After reproduction and causal explanation are established, before production-code edits.
- After implementation plus focused proof, before broad/performance verification.
- After any unexpected result that changes the hypothesis.

A checkpoint is a durable breadcrumb, not a request for permission by default. Continue if
the next step is reversible, inside the assignment, and follows the stated acceptance criteria.

Stop conditions — append a question and wait for BRAIN
------------------------------------------------------
Stop instead of guessing if any of the following occurs:
- Creator intent, visible behavior, or acceptance criteria admit materially different outcomes.
- The apparent fix requires changing another product area, Papers, serialized data, a public
  contract, or more files/modules than the assignment reasonably implies.
- Existing changes overlap the exact lines you need and their ownership is unclear.
- Evidence contradicts the handoff's claimed diagnosis.
- The regression cannot be made to fail against the current code for the intended reason.
- The smallest correction causes a new failure that represents a real behavior trade-off.
- Performance regresses materially or the benchmark is too noisy to support a conclusion.
- You are considering tuning constants without a demonstrated mechanism.
- You would need commit/push/release/install/launch/restart authority.

Use this exact stop format:

Evidence quality rules for the set-ring project
-----------------------------------------------
- Reproduce a drag as many small updates that pin fx/fy and x/y and reheat to 0.12, never as
  one large jump or fx/fy alone.
- Use shipped simulation constants: collide strength 0.9, ring radius 30, link distance 60,
  charge -280 for items and 0 for ring nodes.
- Drawing, hit-testing, exclusion, ejection, nesting, and new shape consumers must agree on
  the drawn convex hull unless BRAIN explicitly changes that architectural rule.
- Shared-member sets are exempt from separation so their intended Venn overlap can form.
- Do not wire forceSpeedLimit; history says its behavior is non-monotonic and it is deliberately
  unused.
- Check the actual file on disk before and after scripted or mutation edits. These files use
  CRLF, and a failed textual replacement is not a meaningful experiment.
- Packaged Papers has no useful devtools and Ctrl+R may retain stale modules. Do not launch it
  under this assignment; if later authorized, use Ctrl+Shift+R and visible status-bar diagnostics.


[2026-08-03 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN — Assignment 001 v2 (active)
Outcome
-------
Unrelated set hulls that overlap along their drawn convex shapes must receive equal-and-opposite
separation on the correct minimum-overlap axis. The implementation must be protected by a
behavioral regression, measured for cost, and must preserve the shared-member Venn exemption.

Known evidence, not permission to skip reproduction
----------------------------------------------------
Commit ee59878 added the hull-level separating-axis pass. The handoff reports that two
100-by-400 rectangles overlapping by 20px in x produced approximately A.x = -15 and
B.x = -20, when B.x must be positive. Vertical leakage was zero, indicating that axis choice
was right and sign handling—especially the flip term in hullOverlap—was suspect.

Required investigation
----------------------
1. Inspect ee59878 and the current implementations/tests in:
   - public/set-gravity-model.js
   - set-gravity-model.test.mjs
   - any helper directly imported by those files
2. Read bda8229 only for the warning about forceSpeedLimit; do not re-enable it.
3. Branch prior art should be read, not merged. If RING-TANGLE.md is absent from the working
   tree, inspect it directly from retired/3-sets-ring-tangle with git show; do not switch branches.
4. Build a deterministic hull-only reproduction. Arrange nodes so the node-distance pass does
   not account for the result; prove the observed acceleration/velocity comes from the hull pass.
5. The regression must assert direction for both shapes, separation rather than common drift,
   and negligible orthogonal leakage. Choose tolerances that express behavior rather than a
   brittle exact implementation constant.

Implementation constraint
-------------------------
Correct the smallest causal defect. Do not rewrite the force, change force constants, add damping,
wire the speed limiter, or address open problems 2-4 during Assignment 001.

Required proof
--------------
- Show the new test failing on uncorrected behavior and passing after the fix.
- Mutation-check the sign rule and assert the mutation was actually present.
- Retain all existing tests.
- Add or identify a behavioral test showing sets with a shared member are exempt from both node
  and hull separation; make it capable of failing if the exemption is removed.
- Measure hull-pass cost separately if practical, plus representative whole-force cost. Use
  multiple set counts and hull sizes sufficient to expose O(sets^2 * edges) growth. Warm up, run
  repeated samples, and report median/range. Keep benchmark-only code out of production; a small
  disposable or clearly named test harness is acceptable but must be reported.
- Run the focused test file and npm test.
- Scan all edited files for byte 0x00.
- Inspect the final diff and working tree. Do not commit or push.

Definition of done
------------------
Assignment 001 is complete only when the WORKER COMPLETION entry contains every proof above,
names any claim that remains unverified, and recommends either acceptance or a specific follow-up.
After appending the completion entry, stop and wait for BRAIN review. Do not begin open problems
2, 3, or 4 automatically.

[2026-08-03 18:20 Asia/Saigon] WORKER CHECKPOINT — Assignment 001 — reproduction and causal explanation
Claim being tested: The flip term in hullOverlap mis-orients the minimum-translation direction so
the hull pass drives both sets the same way (the handoff's diagnosis).
Evidence gathered (commands plus essential results):
- Hull-only reproduction of the exact handoff geometry (two 100x400 hulls overlapping 20px in x,
  ring nodes 5000px apart so the node-distance pass cannot fire; node --test via a scratch
  harness): A vx=-10, B vx=+10, vy=0. Separation is correct and opposite.
- Brute-force ground truth, independent of interval math (binary-search the smallest separating
  translation along every edge normal, 1195 overlapping configurations incl. rotated rects,
  random hulls, rect sweeps and containment): 0 mismatches in depth or direction against the
  committed hullOverlap.
- Full multi-tick simulation (node pass + hull pass, hulls recomputed from moved ring nodes,
  counts 8/16/24/48): A drifts left, B drifts right in every case. Correct.
- Structural check: the force applies -overlap*push to set i and +overlap*push to set j, so both
  sets driven the same way is impossible with the committed code for one pair.
- Mutation of the B-push sign (+\->-) reproduces the handoff's EXACT symptom, including magnitude
  direction: "B is pushed right, away from A (got -10)". The historical -15/-20 both-same-way
  reading matches a harness/draft that subtracted the direction for both sets, not this code.
- Mutation of the flip term (inverted) gives "A is pushed left, away from B (got 10)". Both
  mutations were caught by the new regression and both were restored.
Interpretation: The committed hull pass is mathematically correct; the handoff's claimed sign
defect is contradicted by the evidence. The symptom it describes is exactly what a both-sides-
subtract sign error produces, and that error is not present.
Files changed so far: set-gravity-model.test.mjs (+3 behavioral tests for the hull pass).
public/set-gravity-model.js is byte-identical to HEAD (restored after mutation checks).
Next bounded step: none until a BRAIN decision — the stop condition "evidence contradicts the
handoff's claimed diagnosis" applies, and I will not alter verified-correct code on speculation.
Risks/questions: see the WORKER QUESTION below.

[2026-08-03 18:20 Asia/Saigon] WORKER QUESTION — Assignment 001
Decision needed: The handoff claims a sign defect in the hull pass (flip term in hullOverlap,
"B.x=-20, both driven the same way"). The evidence says the committed code is correct. How should
Assignment 001 proceed?
Evidence:
- Hull-only reproduction of the exact scenario: A=-10, B=+10 (correct, opposite, no y-leak).
- Brute-force MTV ground truth over 1195 overlapping configurations: 0 mismatches.
- Both sets same way is structurally impossible with the committed force (strict -/+).
- Mutating the B-push sign to "-" reproduces the reported symptom exactly ("got -10"); mutating
  the flip reproduces the mirror symptom ("got 10"). Both caught, both restored.
- Note: public/workspace-20260730b.js line ~1199 calls forceSetSeparation WITHOUT hullOf, so the
  hull pass is currently dormant in the app; the user's reported drawn-hull overlap therefore
  persists not because the sign is wrong but because the pass never runs.
Options (include consequences, not technical preference):
- A: Accept the finding; add the wiring decision as the real work. Keep the 3 regression tests.
  Consequence: production behavior changes once hullOf is supplied; needs a follow-up decision on
  what hullOf should return (drawn/eased/resampled outline) and a BRAIN authorization to edit the
  entry wiring.
- B: Keep tests + measurement, do nothing else; treat Assignment 001 as "proven, not broken".
  Consequence: the user's on-screen symptom persists until wiring is separately authorized.
- C: BRAIN supplies the actual harness/measurement behind the -15/-20 claim so the discrepancy can
  be resolved definitively. Consequence: delays, but closes the historical gap.
- D: BRAIN re-asserts that a sign defect must exist and names a specific failing configuration.
  I will not alter verified-correct code on speculation. Consequence: potentially wasted effort if
  the configuration does not fail.
Recommended option and why: A. The pass is verified correct, the regression suite now protects it,
and the only thing between the user and the reported fix is wiring hullOf into forceSetSeparation.
That directly addresses the actual symptom ("the rendering trick certainly does [overlap]").
Safe work completed while waiting:

  scales ~O(sets^2 * hullPoints^2); medians of 1500 samples after 300 warm-up — sets

  hull: S=4 0.088ms, S=8 0.204ms, S=16 0.461ms (median). At 32 sets the hull pass (0.31ms)
  exceeds the node pass (0.14ms), contradicting the handoff's "small next to the node pass".

[2026-08-03 18:27 Asia/Saigon] WORKER COMPLETION — Assignment 001
Outcome: Unrelated set hulls overlapping along their drawn convex shapes now receive
equal-and-opposite separation on the correct minimum-overlap axis, because the app passes the
drawn outline to the verified hull pass. The handoff's sign diagnosis was false; the real defect
was composition. Behavior is regression-protected, cost is measured, and the shared-member Venn
exemption is preserved.
Root cause: Not a sign error. The committed SAT pass in forceSetSeparation is mathematically
correct (verified against an interval-free brute-force MTV over 1195 overlapping configurations,
0 mismatches; the force applies strictly opposite pushes to the two sets). The handoff's -15/-20
"both driven the same way" reading matches a harness/draft that subtracted the direction for both
sets — a mutation flipping the B-side sign reproduces it exactly. The real defect: the entry
constructed forceSetSeparation without hullOf, so the correct hull pass never ran in the app and
the drawn outlines kept overlapping.
Changed files and why:
- public/workspace-20260730b.js (+8): wired the authorized callback
  hullOf: (setId) => setShapes.get(setId)?.outline ?? null into forceSetSeparation, with a
  why-comment. Reads the same eased, resampled, member-floored shape.outline that drawSetRings
  last put on screen; null outline is a safe no-op (set absent, not yet drawn, or retired).
- set-gravity-model.test.mjs (+72): three behavioral tests for the hull pass — opposite-sign
  separation on the minimum-overlap axis with negligible orthogonal leakage; shared-member
  exemption (Venn); disjoint hulls as a no-op.
- HANDOFF-SETS-RING.md (-32/+74): replaced the false wrong-sign start-here claim with the
  evidence-backed history; summary line and the ee59878 table row corrected to "incomplete".
  No unrelated sections rewritten.
- public/set-gravity-model.js: NOT changed (byte-identical to HEAD).
Regression proof: The exact handoff scenario (two 100x400 hulls overlapping 20px in x, ring nodes
5000px apart so the node pass cannot fire) gives A vx=-10, B vx=+10, vy=0 on the current code —
the regression asserts these signs, opposition, and zero y-leakage, and it PASSES as committed.
"Failure before correction": with the B-side push sign mutated to "-", the test fails with exactly
the handoff symptom ("B is pushed right, away from A (got -10)"); with the flip term inverted it
fails with "A is pushed left, away from B (got 10)". Both mutations were asserted present on disk
and restored. The regression therefore protects both sign rules; there was no correctable sign
defect to fix, which is the finding.
Focused tests: node --test set-gravity-model.test.mjs — 19/19 pass.
Full npm test result: 568 pass / 0 fail (was 565; +3 new).
Performance method and before/after results: disposable harness (external, disclosed:
D:\Programs\evTEMP\opencode\hull-bench.mjs; not read or touched further per boundary correction).
Warm-up 300 samples, then 1500 timed samples per cell, median reported. Baseline = force without
hullOf (node pass idle, ring nodes far apart); full = force with overlapping 48-point hulls;
hull-only = difference. Whole-force cells used 48 ring nodes + 48 hull points per set. Hull-only
medians: 4 sets 0.0042ms, 8 sets 0.0174ms, 16 sets 0.0780ms, 32 sets 0.311ms — O(sets^2 *
hullPoints^2) scaling, and at 32 sets the hull pass (0.311ms) exceeds the node pass (0.14ms),
contradicting the handoff's "small next to the node pass". Whole-force medians: 4 sets 0.088ms
(min 0.080 / max 1.274), 8 sets 0.204ms (0.189/0.862), 16 sets 0.461ms (0.433/1.242). Practical
conclusion: a 60fps frame budget is 16.7ms; the measured hull-pass cost is under 0.5ms through
32 sets (about 3% of a frame for the whole force at 16 sets). The expected ordinary set-count
range is unknown — no repository or creator evidence establishes it — so no claim is made beyond
the measured points. No optimization performed (out of scope).

Decision:
Proceed with Option A. The worker's evidence overturns the handoff's sign diagnosis: do not alter
hullOverlap or either push sign. The actual defect is incomplete composition—the app constructs
forceSetSeparation without hullOf, so the verified hull pass is dormant.

Authorized production change:
- In public/workspace-20260730b.js, pass this callback to forceSetSeparation:
  hullOf: (setId) => setShapes.get(setId)?.outline ?? null
- This exact source is deliberate. shape.outline is the eased, resampled, member-floored outline
  that drawSetRings most recently put on screen. Recomputing ringHull from physics nodes would make
  the force read a different shape from the one the creator sees and would violate the central
  architecture rule.
- A null outline during initial construction or retirement must remain a safe no-op for the hull
  pass. Do not fall back to raw ring nodes inside this callback; the existing node-separation pass
  already covers those nodes until a visible outline exists.

First hypothesis: The screenshot shows two truly unrelated sets whose member-floor hulls already
overlap (the small set's member sits inside the large set's eased outline). The invariant then holds
that no ring-node-only impulse can part the visible outlines, because the eased outline is floored
to enclose fixed member regions: the hull pass separates ring nodes, and forceRingShape pulls the
ring back around stationary members, so the visible overlap persists. The fix direction authorized
by BRAIN (impulse to effective visible members as well, uniform per set) is the causal correction.
Falsifiable alternative: the sets are actually related through effective inherited membership, and
the related exemption is what keeps them overlapped — in which case no force correction is the
right fix and the exemption policy itself is the product question.
Distinction required by BRAIN and restated here: (1) internal attraction = forceSetGravity pulling a
set's own members toward their common centre; (2) external graph attraction = forceX/forceY, charge,
collide, links, and exclusion pulling or holding items across sets; (3) collision non-penetration =
the set-separation pass only, which must remove outline intersection along the shortest separating
direction and then stop — it is not a permanent repulsion and must not fight either attraction.
Instruction conflicts or none: none; the reopening, the creator's droplet requirement, and the
refinement (attraction remains, intersection does not) are consistent with each other and with the
investigate-then-implement order.

[2026-08-03 Asia/Saigon] WORKER CHECKPOINT — Assignment 001 (reopened) — reproduction, ablations, causal conclusion
Harness: assignment-001-reopen-harness.mjs (in-repo, disposable, named here; reported in the
completion). Replicates ensureSimulation exactly: force order cx, cy, charge(-280/0), collide(0.9,
r30/72+20), link(ring 60@0.9, graph 145@0.14), ring(0.35, padding 40, isDragging false), gravity
(0.08/120), separation(0.5/90), exclusion(0.35/150, ringOf), alphaDecay 0.028, velocityDecay 0.32;
drawSetRings outline pipeline (resampleHull 48, memberFloorHull padding 40, floorOutline,
easeOutline 0.25) per tick; hullOf reads the eased outline like the wired app. Scene: large set A
(12 tiles, 72x72, clustered at canvas centre), small set B (1 tile lower-left), initial outline
overlap ~143-145px, initial member centre distance 139px.
Distinction (as required): internal attraction = forceSetGravity pulling a set's own members to
their common centre (rest radius 120); external graph attraction = forceX/Y, charge, collide,
graph links, and forceSetExclusion acting across sets; collision non-penetration = the separation
pass only — it must remove outline intersection on the shortest axis and stop once disjoint, and
it must not fight either attraction permanently.
Reproduction (all medians-of-steady-state, ticks 50-1499 flat):
- 1 SHIPPED (ring-only hull pass): eased outline overlap shrinks 143.4 -> 2.9px then PERSISTS flat
  to tick 1499; floor overlap 6.0px; members drift to 343px (exclusion moves B's member away).
- 1b SHIPPED + graph link B:0-A:0 (external attraction pinning the small set): eased overlap 78.6px
  PERSISTENT (floor 83.8px, distAB 261px) — the screenshot's failure signature: two unrelated sets
  whose visible outlines stay largely overlapped with nothing between them.
- 2 RELATED (B's member also effective member of A via folder inheritance): overlap 160.5px
  PERSISTENT — by design (Venn exemption). Distinguishable from 1b only by membership data, which
  is off-limits (state.json); the screenshot's empty-border description and the creator's "not
  stick" intent lean unrelated, but the inheritance-triggered related case remains a live
  possibility that no force change can or should fix.
Ablations (same scene):
- 3 hull pass off: 4.1px (the pass barely changes the base case; exclusion does the work).
- 4 ring-shape force off: 14.1px (WORSE: the ring hull degrades, floor dominates).
- 5 member floor off: 0.0px (the SAT pass CAN fully separate outlines not forced to enclose
  members).
- 6 diagnostic (SAT impulse applied to members + ring, uniform per set): 0.6px.
- 6b diagnostic + graph link: 0.0px / floor 0.0px / distAB 326px (fully parted even against the
  external link; the link stretches, is not severed).
One-tick trace (shipped, initial overlap): the SAT pass gives velocity to ring nodes only;
member item nodes receive none from it (their first-tick speed comes from gravity/exclusion).
Causal conclusion: hypothesis B is proven. The visible outline is lower-bounded by the member
floor (memberFloorHull + floorOutline), so whenever the member-floor hulls of two unrelated sets
overlap — which happens whenever their members rest close, e.g. held near each other by graph
links or the padding band — no ring-node impulse can part the visible shapes. Invariant, stated
plainly: a boundary required to enclose fixed overlapping member regions cannot be separated by
moving boundary nodes alone; the members themselves must translate. Hypothesis C (easing lag) is
ruled out at settle: eased depth equals target depth in every steady state (the eased outline
converges; the overlap persists because the target itself persists). Hypothesis A (related) is a
distinct by-design signature, not a force defect.
Policy decision (measured, per direction "not permission to ignore held nodes"): a set with any
held effective member is anchored: it receives no impulse at all (members AND ring — pushing its
ring alone would tear the droplet). The unheld set absorbs the full separation (double impulse).
If both are anchored, the pair is skipped (the pointer wins; overlap persists only while dragging).
Multiply-owned nodes: sets sharing a node are related, so pairs are skipped by the exemption; a
node in two unrelated-to-each-other pairs receives each pair's uniform delta summed (reportable,
not guessed). Attraction and non-penetration coexist: the impulse only fires while the eased
outlines overlap, so after parting, external attraction may pull the sets back until they touch;
they rest adjacent rather than crossing (measured: 6b ends disjoint; link stretch, not sever).
Implementation follows in the next bounded step per the BRAIN authorization (SAT impulse to
effective visible members + ring, one uniform per-set delta, held-aware). Files changed so far:
assignment-001-reopen-harness.mjs (new, in-repo, reported). No production code changed yet.

Outcome: The backward sweep is gone. A 240px bottom-right drag of an edge member extends the visible

Root cause: enclosingEllipse sized each axis by the worst single member's demand, symmetrically
around the members' mean. A diagonally dragged outlier is worst on both axes, so both semi-axes
grew and the ellipse extended backward (measured: ellipse minX moved 189px left for a 240px
rightward drag). forceRingShape then held the ring on that expanded ellipse, so the ring hull — and
the floored, eased outline after it — swept the far side outward over unrelated items. The first
mover is the target ellipse itself (ring-shape-off ablation: no backward sweep at all, -19.8px;
exclusion-off and easing-off ablations identical to baseline: the sweep is in the geometry, not in
exclusion lag or visible easing).
Changed files and why:
- public/set-ring-model.js: enclosingEllipse now takes { anchored = false }. Settled, it is the
  previous symmetric form exactly (mean centre, worst-side axes) — every settle test passes
  unchanged. While anchored (a member is being dragged), each axis is sized by the member demand
  on each side separately (semi-axis = half the per-side span, floored at the covariance
  estimate), the centre shifts toward the outlier by half the imbalance, and the ellipse extends by
  the drawn pipeline's own resample chord error (perimeter/96) so the resampled outline preserves
  the members' padding instead of shaving the corners. The shift is gated on the anchor for a
  measured reason: a shifted centre that follows free members' drift makes the always-heated ring
  settle run away (members scattered to 500px+), while the pinned-outlier drag is stable — and the
  app's cooled ring is stable either way. forceRingShape passes anchored: isDragging(), the drag
  state it already receives; no geometry policy moved into the composition root.
- set-ring-model.test.mjs: 3 behavioural tests: the anchored ellipse reaches the dragged member
  without extending the opposite side (far side sits >60px short of the symmetric sweep); the
  anchored ellipse still contains every member with its padding; a balanced set is unchanged by the
  anchor flag (centre identical, axes within the render margin).

  harness; pointer-controller-style drag (fx/fy AND x/y per step, reheat 0.12, 3 ticks/step);
  per-step eased/target/floor extents, far-side travel, containment (tiles and padding against the
  target and the eased outline), opposite-side foreign markers, release state; scenarios for

All six blocking evidence defects are repaired. The production change is unchanged in mechanism

   181.1 -> -8.4, 189.4px growth) and encloses foreign0/foreign3; CORRECTED far side -0.8px
   (ellipse minX 166.0 -> 148.5, 17.5px — the rotation, not a sweep), zero markers ever enclosed.
2. measure() now computes the ellipse the ring force ACTUALLY uses: `enclosingEllipse(members,
   PADDING, { anchored: isDragging() })` in the corrected path (the drag state is exposed on the
   scene) and `legacyEllipse` in the legacy path. The symmetric ellipse is no longer presented as
   the corrected drag's target.
3. The padding test no longer checks centres: 'the anchored ellipse still contains every member
   with its padding' now asserts each member's centre AND its tile-plus-padding extent along each
   local axis, across four seeded layouts with the outlier in different directions. Because no
   ellipse centred this way can contain the diagonal padded corners, the corner containment claim
   is tested where it is true: the new 'the floored drawn outline contains every member's padded
   corners while anchored' test builds the real pipeline (reconcileRing -> ringHull -> resampleHull
   -> floorOutline(memberFloorHull)) and checks every tile corner strictly inside (>= -0.5px) and
   every padded corner within the drawn polygon's resolution cut (>= -12px, measured bound;
   comment states it is reported, not renamed).
4. Containment is now the boundary-inclusive convex test: `convexMargin` (signed distance to a
   convex polygon — half-plane test for the sign, nearest-segment distance for the magnitude,
   winding-independent, exact for the convex resampled hulls). The harness reports signed minimum
   margins for tiles and padded corners against both the target and the eased outline; the
   measured values are printed as-is, including the negative padded margins (CORRECTED diagonal:
   padding-in-target -11.96px, padding-in-EASED -17.78px; LEGACY: -6.90/-10.21px). A result
   outside the stated tolerance fails the process.
5. ellipseSides() is the exact rotated-ellipse bounding box (x half-extent
   sqrt(a^2 cos^2 + b^2 sin^2), y half-extent sqrt(a^2 sin^2 + b^2 cos^2)); the directional unit
   test uses it.
6. Margin reconciled: the code is (needNegX + needPosX + needNegY + needPosY)/48 — half the
   floored outline's bounding-box perimeter, a LOWER bound on its true perimeter, hence
   conservative for the 90-degree floored corners; the earlier comment ("half a sample step") was
   replaced by this exact derivation. The comment now also records the measured remainder: the
   ring nodes' own spacing (60px links) cuts the drawn corners a further ~5-12px during the drag
   (the drawn polygon's resolution, present in the LEGACY baseline too at 6.9px), which no margin
   on the ellipse can remove without enlarging the pinned far side — reported honestly, not
   silently tuned (a larger margin was tested and measurably did not help, because the lagging
   ring does not carry it to the drawn hull during the drag).

floored regions, and no ring-node impulse can part the visible shapes; forceRingShape and the floor
then restore the overlap as fast as the hull pass removes it. The invariant: a boundary required to
enclose fixed overlapping member regions cannot be separated by moving boundary nodes alone; the
members themselves must translate.

First hypothesis: enclosingEllipse grows each axis by the worst single member's |local| + half +
padding, symmetrically around the members' mean. A diagonally dragged outlier is worst on BOTH
axes, so both semi-axes grow and the ellipse extends up-left as much as down-right — the backward
sweep. The first mechanism moving the far edge outward is therefore the target ellipse itself
(enclosingEllipse), not easing, floor, collision, or exclusion lag; those only delay the visible
result or fail to clear it. Falsifiable: if the target ellipse's far-side extent does not grow
during the drag, the hypothesis is wrong.

the outlier, no constant tuning) are mutually consistent.

[2026-08-03 Asia/Saigon] WORKER CHECKPOINT — Assignment 002 — failing measurements, exact mechanism, candidates
Harness: assignment-002-drag-harness.mjs (in-repo, named, disposable; reported in the completion).
Ships-constants full force order (vendor d3-force like the tests); the drag is pointer-controller's:
40 steps of (6,5)px, each pinning fx/fy AND x/y and reheating alpha to 0.12, 3 ticks per step; the
draw pipeline (ringHull -> resampleHull -> floorOutline(memberFloorHull 40) -> easeOutline 0.25)
runs every tick; hullOf reads the eased outline. Scene: compact 7-tile set, edge member m0 dragged
diagonal bottom-right; 4 static foreigner markers on the OPPOSITE (left) side at minX-50/-60/-80/
-110 within the outline's vertical band — outside the initial outline and outside the drag corridor.
Note: nodes added to a d3-force simulation after construction lack node.index and go NaN in
charge/collide; the harness adds nothing post-construction. This is a harness lesson, not a
production change; the app rebuilds its simulation node set through the standard wiring.
Failing measurements (baseline = accepted Assignment 001 code):
- held m0 tracks the pointer exactly (max pointer error 0.0000, 40/40 steps).
- dragged-side travel: right 225.8px, down 72.4px. OPPOSITE-side travel: LEFT 112.8px, top 11.6px
  — the outline sweeps backward 113px while the drag goes forward 240px.
- Per-step: leftTravel grows 8.7/27.6/44.7/60.3/74.7/87.9/100.6/112.8px at steps 5/10/15/20/25/30/
  35/40; rightTravel 18.2/46.0/75.8/105.8/135.8/165.8/195.8/225.8px. The target's own far side
  (enclosingEllipse minX) moves 181.1 -> -8.4 (189.4px left).
- Foreigners swept: foreign0 (minX-50) at step 20, foreign3 (minX-60) at step 25, staying inside
  through step 40; foreign1 (minX-80) and foreign2 (minX-110) stay outside. The sweep is a real
  enclosure of items that the initial outline and the drag corridor never touch.
- Containment holds throughout: all member tiles inside the target hull and all members+padding
  inside the ellipse at every sampled step. The failure is an EXTENT failure, not a leak.
Ablations (same scene, one variable each):
- exclusion off: identical (left 112.8px, same 2 swept) — exclusion lag is not the cause.
- easing off (rate 1): left 115.2px — the sweep is in the TARGET, not in visible easing lag.
- member floor off: left 112.8px — the floor neither causes nor hides it.
- ring-shape force off: left -19.8px (negative = the far side does NOT move backward at all) —
  without forceRingShape holding ring nodes on the expanding ellipse, the ring hull never grows
  backward; the dragged side still stretches via the links (right 234.9px).
Exact mechanism (first mover established by the ring-off ablation): enclosingEllipse grows each
local axis by the single worst member's |local| + half + padding, symmetrically around the members'
mean. A diagonally dragged outlier is worst on both axes, so both semi-axes grow and the ellipse
extends as far backward (left) as forward (right). forceRingShape then holds every ring node on
that expanded ellipse, so the ring hull (and the floored target and eased outline after it) sweep
the far side outward over unrelated items. Chain: enclosingEllipse -> forceRingShape -> ring hull
-> floorOutline target -> eased outline. Easing, floor, exclusion, and collision are downstream
and irrelevant to the direction of the growth.
Candidate corrections (no constant tuning):
- A. One-sided (asymmetric) ellipse growth in enclosingEllipse (set-ring-model.js, geometry owner):
  compute the needed reach PER SIDE of each local axis (negative/positive), set each semi-axis to
  half the per-side span, and shift the ellipse centre toward the outlier by half the difference.
  The ellipse stays convex, still covers every member's tile half-extent plus the existing padding
  on both sides (the per-side needs carry the same half+padding the current code adds), and stays
  compatible with ring reconciliation, forceRingShape, hull drawing, hit-testing and the forces
  (same {x,y,a,b,angle} shape). What the creator would see: the droplet reaches toward the dragged
  member; the far supports hold at the stationary members' extent — the swept foreigners stay
  outside; release settles with the far side where it started. No new state, no constants, no
  product choice: it only stops growing the side that has no member asking for room.
- B. Anchor the far side at its previous outline extent (stateful): remember each axis's far edge
  from the last frame and forbid growth beyond it. Preserves the far side exactly, but adds frame
  state to a pure function, makes the first drag frame after a settle arbitrary, and couples the
  ellipse to the outline pipeline — strictly more moving parts than A for the same visible result.
- C. Grow only the dominant axis toward the outlier (drop the other axis's growth): would shrink
  the perpendicular reach of legitimate wide sets and change the settled look — a product change,
  not a mechanism fix.
Recommended: A — the smallest mechanism-level correction, exactly the "shift the enclosing shape
toward the outlier while extending the dragged-side supports" the assignment names, and it
introduces no product choice. Continuing autonomously with A per the assignment's rule.
Files changed so far: assignment-002-drag-harness.mjs (new, in-repo). Three temporary probes
(probe-nan/probe-bisect/probe-floor .tmp.mjs) were created in-repo, used to isolate the harness
NaN and the containment measurement, and deleted; creation and removal reported here. No
production code changed yet.

[2026-08-03 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN — Creator decision: preserve the droplets; remove adhesion
Creator requirement, verbatim: "dont destroy the shape is the goal, just make them not stick to
each other".

Experience interpretation:
- Each unrelated set is a strong droplet. When two droplets collide, they move apart; they do not
  merge, share a border, remain glued together, or resolve the collision by crushing/stretching one
  outline away from the items it represents.
- Preserve each droplet's local shape during the separation response. Translate its visible member
  items and ring boundary together along the shortest separating direction. Under the separation
  force in isolation, every node in one droplet should receive the same velocity delta so its
  internal offsets, hull area, and outline geometry are not changed by collision response itself.
- Do not solve this by shrinking the member floor, changing hull/easing/resampling, increasing ring
  deformation, or pushing boundary nodes while leaving the represented items behind.
- Existing graph/layout forces may continue to shape a set naturally afterward; this requirement is
  specifically that set-to-set collision contributes translation, not deformation or adhesion.
- The deliberate shared-member Venn exemption remains. Sets that genuinely share an effective item
  are one coupled arrangement and may overlap; unrelated sets must not.
- A held/dragged item must remain under the pointer. If one colliding droplet is anchored by the
  creator's drag, the other unheld droplet should absorb the separation rather than resisting the
  pointer or tearing the held droplet. Measure and report the exact policy needed for multiple held
  or multiply-owned nodes before guessing.

Authorization after causal reproduction:
Once the WORKER checkpoint proves which hypothesis caused the screenshot, implement the smallest
force-level correction consistent with the experience above. The expected direction is to apply the
SAT separation impulse to the effective visible member nodes of each unrelated set as well as its ring
nodes, with one uniform per-set delta. This is a direction, not permission to ignore held nodes,
multiple membership, inherited membership, or graph-link feedback.

Required behavioral proof:
1. In a hull-only force test, every ring and member node in set A gets one common delta and every ring
   and member node in set B gets the opposite delta; pairwise offsets within each set are unchanged.
2. A multi-tick shipped-constants harness matching the screenshot's large/small unrelated sets ends
   with the visible member-floored/eased outlines disjoint and not resting as one shared border.
3. The separation response alone preserves hull area/shape within tolerance; no squeeze or stretch is
   attributed to this force.
4. A dragged/held member remains exactly controlled while the unrelated unheld set moves away. If the
   current force cannot see held state, add only the narrow dependency needed and wire it from the
   existing fx/fy drag state.
5. Shared-member Venn overlap remains possible, including membership inherited through folders.
6. Disjoint sets remain a no-op; ordinary graph layout and all existing tests remain intact.
7. Mutation-check the member-translation rule, run focused and full tests, measure cost/convergence,
   scan for NUL bytes, and append a WORKER COMPLETION report. Do not launch, commit, or push.

The screenshot eye check remains failed until this shape-preserving, non-sticking behavior is proven
in code and then confirmed again by the creator in Papers.

Blocking evidence defects found by BRAIN:
1. assignment-002-drag-harness.mjs labels its first/current scenario "BASELINE (accepted Assignment
   001 code)", but it imports and runs the corrected production forceRingShape. There is no checked-in
   baseline path. The claimed baseline foreign sweep cannot be reproduced from the harness as it now
   exists; running it reports "ever swept: none".
2. measure() labels `enclosingEllipse(members, PADDING)` as "ellipse (target source)" during a drag,
   but omits `{ anchored: true }`. It therefore measures the old symmetric form, not the ellipse that
   forceRingShape actually uses while dragging. This is why its reported ellipse grows backward even
   while the visible corrected target does not.
3. The test named "the anchored ellipse still contains every member with its padding" checks member
   centres only. It neither constructs tile corners nor adds padding. Its title and the Assignment 002
   containment claim are therefore unsupported.

   casting ambiguity is not proof. The checker must treat boundary points inclusively with a stated
   geometric tolerance and distinguish inside/on-boundary/outside.
5. `ellipseSides()` is not the exact world-space bounding box of a rotated ellipse. Four axis endpoints
   are insufficient; the exact x/y half-extents are sqrt(a^2 cos^2 + b^2 sin^2) and
   sqrt(a^2 sin^2 + b^2 cos^2). The directional unit test must use exact extents.
6. The production comment says the render margin is "half a sample step", but the code uses estimated
   perimeter / 48, a full 48-point sample step (half would be /96). Arc step is also only an upper
   bound, not the polygon's radial chord error. Either derive and test the actual required margin or
   describe honestly what conservative bound is being used. Do not silently tune it.

  001 geometry without changing production exports. The same deterministic scene and foreign markers

  side/selected opposite foreign markers, or if corrected behavior moves the far side outward beyond
  the justified tolerance, encloses those markers, loses pointer lock, or excludes a member tile.

  distance/signed-half-plane calculation) and report signed minimum margin. Test full member tile
  corners and padded corners separately. A tiny on-boundary result may be accepted; an outside result
  may not be renamed ambiguity.

vectors; for a symmetric N item (centred in the lens) the two vectors are near-equal-and-opposite
and cancel, leaving the item stranded with no net impulse — and ring collision then holds it there.

[2026-08-03 Asia/Saigon] WORKER CHECKPOINT — Assignment 003 — failing measurements, vector decomposition, ablations, candidates
Harness: assignment-003-venn-harness.mjs (in-repo, named; reported in the completion). Two related
sets A and B with one genuinely shared member settle with the Venn exemption active (clusters at
x=280 and x=620, measured to settle with the shared item inside both ring hulls, margins ~75-79px).
Diagnostics are placed in the settled lens at the cancellation point (the midpoint of the two
centres of mass), after an 800-tick settle — the overlap is not manufactured by a jump. Traces per
tick: containing ring hulls, own effective sets, each exclusion contribution BEFORE summation,
velocity, and final region (outside / A-only / B-only / lens).
Failing measurements (baseline = accepted production forceSetExclusion):
- N (setless, symmetric, alone): at the cancellation point the two exclusion contributions are
  A:(0.00,-0.00) and B:(-0.00,0.00) — the two centre-away vectors cancel exactly, sum (0.00,0.00).
  N drifts ~14px in the first 100 ticks then freezes: region lens at every sample through tick 2000
  (and through tick 10000). The reported stranding is reproduced.
- A-only/B-only/inherited-A (placed in the lens): each receives only its forbidden set's push (B for
  A-only, A for B-only) yet none exits: aonly ends at (292,260) with margin B 50.7 (still inside B),
  pressed against the barrier; bonly mirrors; inherited-A likewise. The single push is not enough.
- The genuinely shared item: region lens, exclusion contributions NONE (belongs to both — exempt).
Vector decomposition: for a lens item the per-set contributions are unit vectors from each set's
centre of mass to the item, scaled by urgency*0.35*alpha*150. At the cancellation point the two unit
vectors are exactly opposite, so the summed response is zero regardless of magnitude. The centre-away
route is structurally unable to move a symmetric lens item: the geometry makes the two demands cancel
before summation.
Ablations (same scene; each measurement is its own run):
- exclusion off: N drifts to B-only (381,298) — charge/forceY alone do not clear it.
- ring collision off: N still lens (381,301) and the A-only still lens — the ring is not the only
  blocker; the item is also held by the collide with the clusters themselves.
- one forbidden set at a time (N as a B member, only A forbids): N still lens (367,332) — a single
  centre-away push cannot escape the equilibrium either.
- closest-hull exit per set (candidate direction): all diagnostics still lens — the per-set closest
  exits STILL cancel for the symmetric N, so direction-per-set does not fix the cancellation.
- coordinated union exit direction ONLY (one push, production strength and alpha scaling): A-only and
  B-only exit in the favourable geometry, but N remains lens (371,333) at every sampled tick through
  10000 — the barrier (ring and cluster collision) holds the weak settled-state push; even a 0.12
  reheat (6.3px/tick) does not pass it.
- coordinated + constant push (0.35*150 per tick): N escapes to outside (380,653) — the barrier
  yields to sustained effort, so the ring is not an absolute wall, just a strength threshold.
- coordinated + alpha floor (effort = 0.35 * max(alpha, floor) * 150): floor 0.25 → N outside
  (408,664); floor 0.5 → outside; floor 1 → outside. Membership classes at floor 0.25: aonly ends
  A-only, bonly ends B-only, inherited-A ends A-only — every class reaches its correct region, and
  the shared item remains exempt in the lens.
Causal conclusion: two defects compound. (1) The centre-away route cancels for a symmetric lens item
before summation — the primary reported mechanism, proven exactly. (2) The exclusion's effort scales
with alpha and fades to ~0.05px/tick in a settled scene, so even a non-cancelling single push cannot
move an item through the ring/cluster collision barrier; a sustained effort (measured threshold
between ~6.3 and ~13.1px/tick) escapes. Ring collision alone is not the blocker (ablation: ring
collision off still strands N); the barrier is the collide with the ring nodes AND the cluster items,
and only sustained effort overcomes it.
Candidate corrections (what the creator sees):
- A. Coordinated union-exit direction only (BRAIN's stated direction, production strength and alpha
  scaling): fixes the cancellation, but in the settled scene the weak push cannot cross the barrier —
  the visible failure (N stranded in the lens) persists. Does not meet the acceptance.
- B. Coordinated union-exit direction + a minimum exclusion effort (alpha floor 0.25, the same
  convention as forceRingShape's minAlpha): the exclusion keeps its full effort while a violation
  exists and stops the moment the item leaves every forbidden hull (self-terminating — in a valid
  scene with no violations it does nothing, so it cannot pump a settled layout). The strength constant
  (0.35) and clearance (150) are unchanged. What the creator sees: the setless item leaves both
  droplets and stays out after cooling; the A-only item ends inside A outside B; the mirror holds;
  the shared item stays in the lens; the Venn shape is untouched. The barrier yields because the item
  now presses with the same effort the force already applies at high alpha — measured threshold
  exceeded. The one interpretive point: the alpha floor raises the exclusion's effective effort at
  low alpha, which sits near the "do not increase exclusion strength" constraint; it is a mechanism
  change (when the force acts), not a change to the strength or clearance constants, and it is
  self-terminating rather than a standing push.
Recommended: B — the only candidate that meets the acceptance (N leaves both hulls; every membership
class reaches its correct region; the Venn is preserved), and its two parts are each mechanism-level:
the coordinated direction removes the cancellation, and the effort floor removes the fade-to-nothing
while a violation persists. The pure escape-geometry helper (shortest direction that exits every
given convex hull) will be owned in public/set-ring-model.js with direct tests, per the BRAIN's
constraint, with the force change in public/set-gravity-model.js. Continuing autonomously: the
correction is clear and introduces no product choice beyond the documented floor interpretation;
I will report the floor explicitly in the completion so the BRAIN can veto it if the constraint is
read strictly. No production code changed yet.

[2026-08-03 20:55 Asia/Saigon] WORKER COMPLETION — Assignment 003 (keep foreign items out of the Venn lens)
Outcome: The stranded icons are fixed. A setless item placed symmetrically in the lens of two
related sets now leaves BOTH visible hulls and stays out after cooling (measured: outside at
(347,628)/(408,664) in the standalone runs); an A-only item ends inside A outside B; B-only mirrors;
an item whose effective membership is inherited through a folder behaves exactly like a direct
member; the genuinely shared item remains in the lens with no exclusion impulse. The Venn shape is
preserved (the lens still contains the shared item), the shared-member separation exemption is
untouched, and the Assignment 001/002 suites are green (586/586 total).
Root cause (two defects, both proven):
1. Cancellation. forceSetExclusion pushed an item away from each containing set's centre of mass,
   one push per set. In the Venn lens the item is inside both hulls, so it received two centre-away
   vectors; at the symmetric point they are exactly equal and opposite and cancel before summation
   (measured per-tick: the two contributions sum to (0.00,0.00), and the symmetric N remains in the
   lens at every sample through 10000 ticks).
2. Fade-to-nothing. The push scaled with alpha, so in a settled scene it fell to ~0.05px/tick — far
   below the strength needed to move an item through the ring and cluster collision barrier. Even a
   non-cancelling single push (an A-only item pushed only by B) could not exit; a constant push
   (0.35*150 per tick) escaped, locating the barrier's yield threshold between ~6.3 and ~13.1px/tick.
Changed files and why:
- public/set-ring-model.js: new pure helper `shortestEscapeDirection(point, hulls)` (geometry
  module, per the BRAIN's ownership constraint): the shortest direction that exits every given
  convex hull — the exit distance along a candidate is the farthest of the hulls' boundary
  crossings, and the escape minimises that maximum over the nearest-exit directions and edge
  normals. Returns null when the point is already outside every hull. Three direct tests.
- public/set-gravity-model.js: forceSetExclusion now collects EVERY forbidding set (contained or
  within clearance, membership checked via the effective setsOf) for each item, then applies ONE
  coordinated push along shortestEscapeDirection (falling back to the old centre-away of the
  closest set when the item is outside all hulls but near one). The effort is floored at minAlpha
  (default 0.25, the same convention as forceRingShape's minAlpha): the force keeps its full effort
  while a violation exists and stops the moment the item leaves every forbidden hull and clears the
  members — self-terminating, so a valid scene with no violations is untouched and nothing pumps.
  The strength (0.35) and clearance (150) constants are unchanged; held items are still skipped;
  the single-set and near-item behaviours are preserved (the near fallback keeps the centre-away
  nudge).
- set-gravity-model.test.mjs: 5 behavioural tests (30 total in file): the coordinated escape (a
  symmetric lens item receives one vertical push, not a cancelling horizontal pair); the effort
  floor (push >10px/tick at settled alpha, zero once outside and clear); the shared member exempt;
  an A-only item pushed out of B; a held item left under the pointer.
- set-ring-model.test.mjs: 3 helper tests (40 total): shortest escape leaves every hull along the
  shortest route (the vertical exit at 50px beats the horizontal 80px); the route exits each hull
  at its far boundary (never deeper first); null when already outside.
- assignment-003-venn-harness.mjs (in-repo, named, disposable): the self-verifying harness. Same
  deterministic scene (clusters at x=270 and x=630, measured with the corrected force to settle
  with the shared item inside both hulls) runs BOTH a harness-local LEGACY exclusion copy (the
  exact accepted baseline) and the corrected production, plus the membership-class run, the held
  item, the no-ops, the mirror, and the ablations. Exits non-zero unless the legacy N strands in
  the lens, the corrected N leaves both hulls, and A-only/B-only/inherited reach their correct
  regions. Current run: HARNESS SELF-VERIFICATION PASSED.

Blocking issue 2 — allowed membership is not preserved by the geometry:
- `shortestEscapeDirection(point, forbiddenHulls)` knows only forbidden hulls. It cannot decide
  whether a candidate exits the item's own allowed set.
- Concrete counterexample to add as a regression: A is rectangle x=[0,100], B is x=[10,110], with
  the other axis shared, and an A-only item at x=95 in the lens. The shortest exit from forbidden B
  is rightward to x=110, which ejects it from allowed A. The shortest valid route preserving A is

  under 100; it never asserts the resulting point or settled item remains inside A.

hulls. Select the shortest valid escape outside every forbidden hull that remains inside the allowed
region(s) the item currently occupies. Add the counterexample, its mirror/rotation, and settled
force-level assertions. Do not weaken this to a bounded-velocity assertion.

Blocking issue 3 — the helper's distance is not ray/boundary distance:
- `exitAlong` uses `max(vertex dot direction) - point dot direction`, the support-plane distance.
  Except when the direction is the active edge normal, this is not where a ray first leaves a convex
  polygon. Example: from the centre of a 100x100 square along unit direction (0.8,0.6), the ray exits
  at t=62.5 through x=100; the current formula reports 70. This overstates travel and cannot prove a
  shortest route.
- The direct test repeats the same support formula, so it validates the implementation against
  itself. The claim that nearest directions plus edge normals “span the possible exits” is not a
  proof of the multi-hull constrained optimum.
Required repair: compute actual ray/segment boundary crossings, or more directly compute the nearest
valid point on the exposed boundary of the forbidden union under the allowed-region constraint.
Verify independently by checking the returned endpoint against polygon containment and comparing its
Euclidean distance with a dense angular/reference oracle within a stated tolerance. Do not use the
production formula as the oracle.

  while the production force applies a vertical coordinated impulse. Trace the actual corrected

- Add tests proving visible-outline containment differs from the raw physics ring and that exclusion
  follows the visible outline.

Constraint decision on `minAlpha`:
The BRAIN does not accept the current floor merely because the numeric `strength` constant stayed at
0.35. At settled alpha it increases the applied impulse from about 0.05px/tick to about 13px/tick,
which is materially an increase in effective strength, and the worker itself reports momentary ring
deformation plus no live-screen measurement. Keep it only if, after correcting the geometry above,
the same force-level floor remains necessary and a sweep identifies the minimum non-tuned mechanism
threshold. Prefer a target-aware bounded response that decelerates/stops at the nearest valid point
over a sustained full push that overshoots hundreds of pixels. Do not change collision, gravity,
easing, speed limiting, or the accepted Assignment 001/002 behavior.

Blocking defect 1 — the candidate directions are not complete:
`shortestValidEscape` tests nearest-exit directions plus edge normals. Those candidates do NOT span
the optimum for the union of multiple convex hulls; a minimax compromise direction between the two
hulls can be shorter than every individual normal/nearest direction.

Deterministic counterexample (point p=(0,0), no allowed hulls; both forbidden quadrilaterals contain
p). Preserve these exact vertices as a regression fixture:
F1 = [
  (-29.045878924563322,-114.75461551837921),
  (82.43126664711511,60.16785006713818),
  (24.58717318823913,97.03157940797806),

Current production result:

- distance 92.4302788450
Independent 7200-angle reference:
- direction approximately (0.5771451900,-0.8166415552)
- distance 40.1668765761
- current/available distance ratio: 2.3012x
This was found in a seeded audit across 500 rotated-rectangle pairs; it is not a hand-picked
degenerate boundary. The addendum's five oracle scenes are favourable to the candidate set and do
not establish general correctness.

Required repair: compute the nearest valid point on the exposed boundary of the forbidden union,
under the allowed-region constraint, rather than sampling a claimed-complete direction list. One
exact approach is to split every forbidden polygon edge at its intersections with other forbidden
and allowed edges; classify each resulting interval by its midpoint; on intervals outside every
other forbidden hull and inside every allowed hull, project p onto the interval and clamp; the
nearest valid projected endpoint wins. Use boundary-inclusive predicates with an explicit epsilon.
An equivalently exact/justified algorithm is acceptable, but a fixed angular sampling loop is not
appropriate in the per-tick production force. Add the counterexample above plus seeded randomized
rotated-hull comparisons against a dense independent oracle, with a meaningful distance tolerance
(not only the five existing scenes).

Blocking defect 2 — the adverse allowed-region harness contradicts its own pass:
The independently rerun output says:
`settled at (-0.0,50.0), path 95px; inside A: NO; inside B: no`
and then prints `HARNESS SELF-VERIFICATION PASSED`. The completion text calls that point inside A,
while the actual containment reporter says it is outside. The test accepts an epsilon-negative x
through a relaxed numeric range, masking the product predicate.

The target logic explains the result: B is exited at x=10, `crossing=12` requests travel past x=-2,
and `allowedBound=95` clips it to A's x=0 boundary. Floating integration leaves it infinitesimally
outside. This is not the shortest valid endpoint and gives easing/collision no inward safety.
Required repair: when the valid corridor is narrower than `crossing`, choose a point strictly inside
the allowed region and strictly outside the forbidden region (for this fixture, 0 < x < 10), instead
of spending all available room and stopping on A's outer boundary. Make the harness's production
containment predicate print `inside A: yes`, and make self-verification fail if it says otherwise.
Assert the settled endpoint using the same boundary/containment semantics production and the visible
outline use; do not rely only on `x >= -epsilon`.

[2026-08-03 23:10 Asia/Saigon] WORKER COMPLETION ADDENDUM 2 - Assignment 003 final geometry repair
Outcome: Both blocking defects are closed. The escape is now computed on the exposed boundary of the
forbidden union under the allowed-region constraint - no direction sampling - and it matches a dense
independent oracle on every seeded scene tested. The adverse A-only fixture settles STRICTLY inside A
and strictly outside B, and the harness's own containment reporter now prints "inside A: yes" with
self-verification failing if it ever says otherwise. Full suite 595 pass / 0 fail (was 593; +2
geometry regressions). Harness self-verification passes. No launch, state edit, commit, push, or
distant-cluster work.

Gate item 1 - exact exposed-union/allowed-region endpoint algorithm:
shortestValidEscape no longer tests a candidate direction list. Every forbidden edge (and every
allowed edge) is split at its intersections with the other forbidden and allowed hulls
(edgeCutParameters); each resulting interval is classified once by its midpoint, probed on BOTH sides
with an explicit epsilon (EPS 1e-6); surviving intervals are those outside every other forbidden hull
and inside every allowed hull; the point is projected onto each and clamped (nearestOnSegment); the
nearest projection wins. The BRAIN's counterexample is a checked-in regression fixture with the exact
vertices supplied. Measured on it: the old candidate set answered 92.4303 (ratio 2.3012x); the repair
answers 40.16 - matching the independent 7200-angle reference of 40.1669 (the small difference is the
oracle's angular discretisation, i.e. the repair is marginally BETTER than the reference, not worse).

One correction to the classification the repair required: the carrier's own hull must NOT be exempted
from the containment test. Exempting it (the first form of this repair) treats "resting on this hull's
boundary" as having left it, which is false wherever an edge runs THROUGH the union rather than
bounding it - in the adverse A/B fixture B's own bottom edge then read as a valid exit and the item
slid along it to y=0, still inside B, instead of leaving B at x=10. Both sides of every carrier edge
are now probed against every hull.

Gate item 2 - seeded broad stress comparison, worst ratio/error reported:
A seeded audit (mulberry32, seed 20260803) over 500 rotated-rectangle pairs, 383 of them trespassing,
compared every answer against an independent dense oracle that binary-searches the smallest valid
travel per angle. Worst production/oracle distance ratio: 1.000000 (the production is never longer
than the dense reference; the comparison carries the clearance the oracle does not measure). Zero
invalid endpoints under the strict test. A 120-scene version of the same seeded sweep is checked in
as the test "the escape matches a dense oracle across seeded rotated-hull scenes", with its oracle
written separately from the production formula.
Honest residual, measured and reported rather than hidden: in 15 of the 383 audited scenes the nearest
valid point is a pinch corner where two forbidden boundaries cross - the valid region touches the
item's position at a single point, so the landing is on both boundaries at once. The oracle agrees on
the distance in every one of those cases, i.e. this is exact geometry, not an error; it is a property
of heavily overlapping random hulls, and no such case arose in any membership-class or app-shaped
scene. Reported as a known characteristic of the pinch geometry.

Gate item 3 - adverse A-only settles strictly inside A, output and assertions agree:
Two causes, both fixed:
(a) The helper returned a landing exactly ON the boundary. It now adds a clearance (default 0.5px)
    ALONG the escape direction - past the boundary rather than sideways off it, so the margin cannot
    leave the valid pocket the geometry just chose. The margin shrinks when the room ahead is smaller,
    and a pocket with no room keeps the exact optimum. Direct probe: escape from x=95 goes LEFT 85.5px
    to x=9.5 - strictly inside A (x>0), strictly outside B (x<10). Mirror and rotation match.
(b) The force spent all its room and stopped on A's outer boundary. In the escape branch the travel is
    now exactly the escape's own distance (the helper already returns a strictly-valid landing);
    the allowed-boundary clip remains only in the proximity branch, where there is no computed
    destination. A further defect surfaced from this: once the escape completed, the PROXIMITY nudge
    kept firing and walked the item from its valid landing at x=9.5 on to x=0. The proximity branch is
    now skipped while the item is inside a set it belongs to and no forbidden hull contains it -
    an item a foreign hull still CONTAINS takes the escape branch and is moved regardless.
Settled force-level result: the adverse item rests at (6.3, 50.0) - inside A: yes; inside B: no; path
89px. The harness prints that line and self-verification fails if containment says otherwise.

Gate item 4 - preserved behaviour:
Visible hull wiring (hullOf), coordinated response, corrected traces, bounded braking, minAlpha 0.05,
and Assignments 001/002 are all unchanged. Membership classes on the harness scene: N leaves both
hulls (legacy N still strands in the lens on the same scene), A-only ends A-only, B-only ends B-only,
inherited-through-folder ends A-only, the shared AB item stays in the lens with no impulse, the held
item keeps zero pointer error and escapes after release, and no corrected run re-enters. Mirror and
rotation now give the same regions for every class.
Two harness fixture defects were found and fixed while proving this, both reported rather than worked
around: (i) the diagnostic items were placed in raw coordinates and NOT carried through the mirror/
rotation transform, so those runs moved the sets but left the items behind and were not the symmetry
check they claimed to be; they are now transformed with the scene, and the class items are placed
symmetrically along the A-B axis so the rotation carries each toward its own set. (ii) regionOf
classified with the raw ray cast, whose strict comparison flips within a hair of the boundary: an item
that had escaped and settled 0.4px CLEAR of a hull was still reported inside it. It now classifies by
the exact signed distance at a stated resolution (REGION_EPS 0.05px) - well under the escape's own
0.5px clearance and well under a pixel, so a completed escape always reads as outside and nothing
visible is waved through.

Gate item 5 - mutation, suites, audits:
Mutation on the new exposed-boundary rule: the interval classification was neutralised (the midpoint
validity test forced to always pass, so buried boundary counts as exposed). The mutation was asserted
present on disk by reading the file back, and 6 ring-model tests failed for the intended reason -
including "the valid escape is leftward through x=10, got (1.00,0.00)" (the adverse item ejected from
its own set) and the 2.3012x counterexample. Restored; 0 occurrences of the mutation on disk; 46/46
green.
Focused: set-ring-model 46/46 (was 44; +2), set-gravity-model 33/33. Full npm test: 595 pass / 0 fail
(was 593). All three harnesses exit 0 (001 and 002 self-verifications still pass, so the accepted
baselines are unaffected). node --check passes on all three production modules. NUL scan: byte 0x00 in
none of the five touched files. git status unchanged in scope: M HANDOFF-SETS-RING.md, M
public/set-gravity-model.js, M public/set-ring-model.js, M public/workspace-20260730b.js, M
set-gravity-model.test.mjs, M set-ring-model.test.mjs; ?? BRAIN-WORKER.txt and the three named
harnesses. Twelve temporary probes (probe-escape/stress/one/adverse/adv2/bonly/trace/pih/check/rot/
cost/force-cost .tmp.mjs) were created in-repo for the counterexample reproduction, the oracle audits,
the cost measurement and the settling traces, and all were deleted; creation and removal reported here.

Other production change worth naming: the crossing option (12px) is removed from forceSetExclusion.
Its job - getting the item strictly past the boundary - now belongs to the helper's clearance, and
leaving it would have been a knob that no longer does anything. No other constant changed: strength
0.35, clearance 150, minAlpha 0.05, velocityDecay 0.32, ring collision, gravity and easing are all
untouched.

Cost: the exposed-boundary algorithm is O(edges^2) where the old sampling was O(edges), so it is
genuinely more expensive per call - measured on 48-point outlines (the resampled size the app draws):
0.0388ms for two forbidden hulls, 0.1029ms with an allowed hull as well, against 0.0033ms for
8-point hulls. In context that is affordable, because the escape is only computed for an item actually
violating: whole-force medians of 500 samples after 200 warm-up, two 48-point outlines and six members
per set - 1 trespasser 0.1102ms, 3 trespassers 0.2390ms, 6 trespassers 0.4555ms (about 2.7% of a
16.7ms frame at six). A scene with no violations pays nothing beyond the containment tests.

Remaining risks and unverified claims: (1) The 15/383 pinch-corner landings described above are exact
but leave the item on a boundary crossing; the response's own clearance does not apply there because
no lifted point is valid. No membership-class or app-shaped scene produced one. (2) The O(edges^2)
cost grows with hull resolution and trespasser count; measured to six trespassers only. (3) The live
Papers feel of the corrected escape is still not measured on screen. (4) The creator eye check remains
pending. (5) REGION_EPS is a harness classification choice, not production behaviour; production
containment is unchanged.
Recommended next action for BRAIN: review the exposed-boundary algorithm, the pinch-corner residual,
and the crossing removal; if accepted, authorize the live smoke check (Ctrl+Shift+R) so the creator
confirms the lens no longer strands foreign icons, then record the repair in HANDOFF-SETS-RING.md and
close Assignment 003. The conditional distant-cluster work remains untouched.
