[2026-08-04 11:18 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN — Assignment 004 (ACTIVE)
CUSTOMIZABLE HOTKEYS + SET IDENTITY RENDERING
======================================================================

Authority and baseline
----------------------
This assignment records creator-approved behavior from the planning conversation on 2026-08-04.
It authorizes implementation and tests inside the independent As you Go Backpack only. It does NOT
authorize an internal/sub-agent, a Papers source or binary change, state.json editing, a Papers
build/release/install/restart, Git staging/commit/push/merge/PR work, or unrelated cleanup.

Expected pickup baseline:
- repository: D:\Letters\MatTroiSeConMoc\Papers\Backpack projects\As you Go
- branch: feat/sets-ring
- head: 58f6c5a (refine warning toast timing and opacity)
- expected working tree: clean; BRAIN-WORKER.txt may be ignored by Git
- current full-suite baseline: 602 passing tests

If the branch/head or pre-existing changes differ, append a WORKER QUESTION before editing. Follow
the BRAIN OPERATING PROTOCOL v2 pickup sequence and append a WORKER PICKUP entry first.

Creator-visible outcome
-----------------------
Build two related capabilities without changing the accepted set physics:

1. The Copy Prompt window gains a separate Hotkeys page. It lists every Backpack-owned keyboard
   action, lets the creator change, clear, and reset its binding, prevents ambiguous conflicts, and
   automatically lists future actions because the page and controllers share one action catalog.

2. Sets gain identity. A selected set can be named. Sets use the same spatial colour behavior as
   folders. An untitled set keeps the existing continuous border. A named set removes the ordinary
   border and replaces it with its name repeated around the complete perimeter in single-stroke,
   ASCII-art-like vector letters. The repeated words are never upside down. The lettering is purely
   decorative: physics, containment, exclusion, hit-testing, Venn membership, ring settling and
   separation continue to use the existing smooth ring/hull exactly as before.

Non-negotiable product decisions
--------------------------------
- This is local As you Go behavior, not a generic Papers hotkey or set framework.
- Planning is complete enough to implement. Do not ask the creator for technical architecture.
- Standard typing behavior inside editable fields and standard accessibility navigation inside
  menus remain native/fixed. The configurable list covers keys that invoke Backpack actions.
- Hotkey changes auto-save. Store only creator overrides, not a copy of every default.
- A binding may be cleared (shown as Unassigned) to disable that action's hotkey.
- Duplicate bindings are blocked only when their actions can be active in the same scope. The same
  key may be used in Workspace and Copy Prompts because those controllers are mutually exclusive.
- Unknown persisted action ids must be preserved and ignored, not silently deleted, so a temporary
  downgrade or removed action cannot destroy creator customization.
- Set colours are automatic, not manually picked. Folders and visible sets share one spatial colour
  field so a nearby folder and set are separated just as two nearby folders are.
- Set names are allowed to be blank. Whitespace-only saves as untitled.
- New sets start untitled. The historical internal placeholder title "New set" was never visible or
  editable; normalize that exact legacy placeholder to untitled so existing rings do not suddenly
  display NEW SET around every border.
- A named set has NO ordinary visible stroke segment. Its vector letter strokes alone describe the
  border. The existing translucent fill remains.
- Repeat the complete name around the full perimeter. Use deliberate spacing between repetitions;
  do not fill gaps with an ordinary border.
- Reorient lower/reversed runs by reversing traversal AND glyph order, so words remain readable
  left-to-right and are neither mirrored nor upside down. Every rendered glyph orientation must be
  normalized to the upright half-plane (equivalent to -90..+90 degrees).
- Anchor the repetition phase deterministically at the top of the current outline so small ring
  deformations do not make the lettering crawl randomly around the set.
- Glyphs never participate in collision, hit-testing, containment, ejection, set separation, ring
  node reconciliation, gravity, or the outline supplied to any physics consumer.
- No SVG <text>, browser font, canvas text rasterization, or ordinary floating label. The visible
  name must be generated from project-owned single-stroke vector glyph geometry.

Phase A — one hotkey action catalog and persistence model
---------------------------------------------------------
First inventory every existing Backpack command binding in:
- public/app/interactions/keyboard-controller.js
- public/app/components/prompt-tree-controller.js
- any other project module with action-owning keydown logic

Do not treat modifier-assisted pointer gestures (Shift drag/pin), editable-control native keys, or
context-menu accessibility navigation as configurable command hotkeys.

Create one catalog consumed by BOTH the Hotkeys page and keyboard controllers. Each definition needs
at least a stable action id, creator-facing label, group, active scope/context, and one or more
defaults. Preserve the existing behavior inventory, including aliases:

Workspace / Sets:
- Escape: dismiss/cancel/clear according to current priority
- G: create a set from the selection
- Ctrl+G: edit the selected items' set memberships
- Ctrl+A, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+X, Ctrl+V
- Ctrl+Z; Ctrl+Y and Ctrl+Shift+Z as redo aliases
- Delete: remove a selected set or delete/bin items according to current priority
- Ctrl+Enter: reveal selection
- Enter: open selection or confirm active membership editing according to current priority
- F2: rename the single selected set (new in this assignment)

Copy Prompts:
- ArrowUp / ArrowDown: row focus
- ArrowLeft / ArrowRight: collapse/expand or parent/child focus
- Enter: open prompt or toggle folder
- F2: edit prompt / rename folder
- Delete and Backspace aliases
- Ctrl+Z; Ctrl+Y and Ctrl+Shift+Z redo aliases
- Ctrl+C, Ctrl+X, Ctrl+V, Ctrl+A
- Escape: current prompt-dialog cancellation priority

Required behavior:
- Canonicalize keyboard events into stable display/persistence strings such as Ctrl+Shift+Z.
- Modifier-only presses are not complete bindings.
- Saved overrides replace the complete default alias list for that action; Reset restores all its
  defaults. This makes custom behavior unambiguous.
- The controllers query current overrides dynamically, not a startup-only stale copy.
- When an action is Unassigned, its old default must no longer fire.
- Preserve native copy/cut/paste/undo/caret behavior inside editable controls exactly as today.
- Do not use DOM/source-code discovery as the catalog. Registration is explicit, and the UI renders
  the registry. Add an architectural test proving an injected/new catalog action renders without a
  second handwritten UI list.

Persistence:
- Keep overrides in project-owned workspace state under one clearly named view/preferences field.
- Normalize valid bindings and preserve unknown action-id values without executing them.
- Resetting the last override removes the empty known-overrides payload when practical, while still
  retaining any unknown records.
- Do not change the host state contract or Papers.

PHASE A CHECKPOINT: append a WORKER CHECKPOINT after the pure catalog/model and regression tests are
green, before changing either controller or building the page. Report the exact action inventory,
state shape, unknown-id policy, conflict model, and tests.

Phase B — Hotkeys page and live controller routing
---------------------------------------------------
Inside the existing Copy Prompt modal, add two clear pages/tabs:
- Prompts (the current tree, unchanged in behavior)
- Hotkeys

Hotkeys page behavior:
- Group actions as Workspace, Sets, and Copy Prompts.
- Each row shows action name, current binding(s), default binding(s), a capture/change control,
  Clear, and Reset. Include Reset all.
- Clicking Change enters an explicit capture state. Display "Press shortcut…". Clicking away or a
  visible Cancel control cancels capture; Escape itself must remain bindable rather than secretly
  cancelling capture.
- On a complete key press, prevent the command from firing while capturing, canonicalize it, check
  conflicts in the same active scope, save it immediately, and re-render the row.
- A conflict does not overwrite either action. Identify the conflicting action by name in local
  modal feedback and leave the capture available for another attempt.
- Successful save and reset feedback stays inside the modal. A failed host save is visible and the
  modal remains open.
- Switching tabs must not discard prompt edits or hotkey changes. Close remains one common action.
- Prompt-tree key handling must be inactive while the Hotkeys page owns capture.
- Keyboard navigation and focus states must remain accessible. The page must be usable without a
  mouse, but ordinary Tab/Shift+Tab navigation remains fixed/native.
- The interface must preserve the warm paper visual language and fit the existing modal without a
  new Papers window or generic settings surface.

Required live behavioral tests:
- A customized workspace binding fires the intended action and its old default does not.
- Clearing a workspace binding disables the old default.
- A customized Copy Prompts binding fires only in the prompt scope.
- Identical keys in mutually exclusive scopes are allowed; same-scope collisions are blocked.
- Capture consumes the key instead of running the underlying command.
- Existing editable-control native behavior stays native.
- Reset one and Reset all restore defaults and persist.
- A newly supplied catalog definition appears in the rendered page automatically.
- Opening/closing/switching pages preserves current prompt-library behavior and data.

PHASE B CHECKPOINT: append a WORKER CHECKPOINT after the Hotkeys page, both controllers, focused
tests, syntax and NUL checks are green. Do not begin set rendering if hotkey persistence or native
editable behavior is uncertain.

Phase C — set naming interaction and shared spatial colours
-----------------------------------------------------------
Naming interaction:
- Exactly one selected set + F2 opens rename editing for that set.
- The selected set's context menu also offers Rename set. Reuse the current set hit-testing and
  selection meaning; do not make decorative glyphs pointer targets.
- Enter confirms, Escape cancels. Blank/whitespace confirms as untitled.
- With zero or multiple selected sets, rename does not guess; show concise status feedback.
- Persist through the existing itemSets state. Preserve ids, members, exclusions, physics state and
  item selection.
- Add Rename selected set to the Phase A catalog with F2 default; do not hard-code a hidden second
  binding path.

Colour behavior:
- Today folder hues are position-derived, eased over time, and separated for nearby folders through
  assignSpatialFolderHues. Extend/reuse that behavior coherently rather than creating an unrelated
  fixed set palette.
- Use one namespace-safe spatial colour map/solver input containing visible folders and visible
  sets. Represent a set at the centroid/centre of its current visible drawn hull; document and test
  the exact choice.
- Moving a set changes its hue smoothly. Nearby set/set and folder/set pairs respect the same hard
  hue-separation invariant as folder/folder pairs.
- Apply the set hue to its translucent fill and either its ordinary untitled border or its named
  glyph strokes. Preserve stronger selected/chosen/partial states by changing alpha/weight, not by
  reverting to green.
- Hidden/temporarily absent sets retain stable session colour state when they reappear, matching the
  folder warm-start behavior. Do not persist colour as creator data.

Required tests:
- New set default and legacy "New set" migration to untitled.
- Rename confirm/cancel/blank, wrong selection counts, and round-trip persistence.
- Folder/set and set/set nearby colour separation; position movement changes hue; unchanged position
  converges; ordering does not affect result.
- Selection/picking states retain the set's own hue.
- No item-set membership or physics output changes merely because title or colour presentation did.

PHASE C CHECKPOINT: append a WORKER CHECKPOINT after naming and shared colours are behaviorally
proven. Record whether the context menu needed a bounded extension and list every state migration.

Phase D — repeated vector-letter perimeter renderer
----------------------------------------------------
Keep two geometries explicitly separate:
1. PHYSICAL/DRAWN BODY: the accepted eased/resampled/floored convex hull used for fill, containment,
   hit-testing and every physics consumer.
2. DECORATIVE BORDER: either the ordinary stroke (untitled) or generated vector glyph strokes
   (named). It is never returned as a hull and never read by physics.

Glyph system:
- Implement a small project-owned, single-stroke monospaced glyph description in normalized
  coordinates. Cover A-Z, 0-9, space and useful common punctuation at minimum. Lowercase input may
  render through the uppercase glyph forms to keep the ASCII-art character. Unsupported Unicode or
  punctuation must render a visible fallback glyph (for example ?) rather than silently vanish.
- Do not put glyph geometry into the force simulation and do not create a DOM node per stroke if a
  compact path per set can express the same result.

Perimeter layout:
- Flatten/sample the current visible outline by cumulative arc length.
- For a non-empty trimmed title, suppress the ordinary stroke and lay out complete title repetitions
  around the full closed perimeter with a consistent inter-word gap.
- Ensure at least one complete title is present. Prefer a readable glyph height; reduce scale only as
  needed when one complete title plus its gap cannot fit. Do not truncate the title.
- Anchor phase at a deterministic topmost outline location with deterministic tie-breaking. A small
  deformation that preserves the set should perturb glyph positions smoothly rather than rotate the
  entire repeating pattern by a word.
- Orient each glyph to the local tangent. Normalize rotations to the upright half-plane. When a run
  crosses into the reversed/downside direction, reverse traversal and the glyph sequence together so
  words still read left-to-right. Never mirror glyph coordinates.
- Handle closed-loop seams without overlapping duplicate glyphs or abruptly changing word order.
- Untitled means an empty/whitespace title and must render the existing continuous border exactly.
- Named means no ordinary border stroke anywhere; only letter strokes describe the perimeter. Keep
  the translucent fill body.
- Fade/retire the glyph border with the set. Selected, picking, chosen and partial states apply the
  same hue with the existing relative alpha/weight emphasis. Glyphs have pointer-events none.

Required pure/layout regressions:
- Untitled returns the existing normal border path and no glyph border.
- Named suppresses the normal stroke and emits vector glyph geometry.
- A sufficiently large ring contains the full title more than once around the perimeter.
- Every emitted glyph rotation is within the upright half-plane.
- A bottom/reversed run spells the word in readable order and does not mirror the glyphs.
- Stable top anchoring under a small outline deformation prevents wholesale phase/crawl.
- Closed seam has no duplicate/overlapping glyph placement.
- Long names render at least one complete, untruncated repetition by scaling.
- Unsupported characters produce the documented fallback glyph.
- Titled versus untitled versions of the same set produce byte-for-byte or tolerance-equivalent
  physical hull, hit-test, exclusion and separation inputs/results.
- Multiple named sets, Venn overlaps, shared members, set retirement and set selection render without
  DOM leaks or stale glyph paths.

Performance evidence:
- Measure glyph layout/path generation on representative 48-point outlines with at least 12 visible
  named sets and both short and long names. Warm up and report median/range. Do not impose a flaky
  wall-clock unit-test threshold, but investigate if the work would consume a material part of a
  16.7ms frame. Cache/recompute only when outline/title/scale changes enough to matter; do not cache
  stale geometry that visibly detaches from a moving ring.

PHASE D CHECKPOINT: append a WORKER CHECKPOINT after pure layout, rendering integration and focused
tests. Include a compact machine-rendered visual fixture (SVG or HTML using production geometry) if
it helps creator review, but do not use ImageGen and do not launch/restart Papers.

[2026-08-04 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN DECISION — Assignment 004 phase pacing; B and C authorized as one run
The creator asked whether the four phases are genuine dependencies or precaution, and authorized a
faster pacing where the dependency is weak. Decision:

Phases B and C may be executed as ONE CONTINUOUS RUN. Build the Hotkeys page and controller routing,
then set naming and shared spatial colours, without pausing between them. Append both the PHASE B
CHECKPOINT and the PHASE C CHECKPOINT as you pass them — they remain mandatory as durable
breadcrumbs and must each still report their own evidence, but neither is a stopping point. The one
real coupling across the seam is that "Rename selected set" (F2) is a Phase A catalog action and must
be registered there, not hard-coded as a hidden second binding path in Phase C.

Phase boundaries that remain hard, with the reason each is genuine rather than precautionary:
- A BEFORE B. The catalog is the load-bearing indirection of the whole hotkey feature. If the page
  and controllers are written while the catalog shape is still moving, the "an injected catalog
  action renders without a second handwritten UI list" test becomes circular — it passes because
  both sides were written to match, not because the indirection is real. Land the pure catalog,
  persistence model and their regressions first, and do not edit either controller before the
  PHASE A CHECKPOINT is appended.
- C BEFORE D. Phase D renders a persisted title around a perimeter. Without real titles in itemSets,
  D must stub a title source and then be rewritten when the real shape lands. The legacy 'New set' ->
  untitled migration also lives in C: shipping D first would display NEW SET around every existing
  ring on the creator's screen.
- D CHECKPOINT BEFORE COMPLETION. Phase D's failure modes are visual and largely invisible to unit
  tests — upside-down or mirrored glyphs, lettering that crawls around the ring as the outline
  deforms, duplicated glyphs at the closed seam, a name that reads correctly in a fixture but not on
  a real deformed hull. The checkpoint is where the machine-rendered visual fixture is produced for
  creator review. Do not fold it into the completion entry.

Standing pacing principle for FUTURE assignments, not only this one:
Phase boundaries in a BRAIN assignment exist for one of two reasons, and the worker may treat them
differently. A boundary is HARD when the later phase would have to be rewritten if the earlier one
turns out wrong, when the later phase depends on data or a contract the earlier one establishes, or
when it is the point at which creator-visible evidence is produced. A boundary is SOFT when the
phases merely group related work and neither constrains the other's shape. Soft boundaries may be
run continuously with checkpoints appended in passing. When a boundary's nature is not stated and
the worker judges it soft, it may proceed continuously — but it must say so in the checkpoint and
name why the later phase could not have forced a rewrite of the earlier one. Checkpoints themselves
are never optional at any pacing: they are the audit trail, and a completion entry that swallows its
checkpoints is not reviewable. The worker still stops on any protocol stop condition regardless of
pacing.

Nothing else in Assignment 004 changes. Scope, acceptance criteria, the completion gate, the
mutation-check list and the out-of-scope list all stand. No code, tests, or documentation were
changed by this entry; no commit, push, branch, build, launch, or state edit occurred.

Exact action inventory: Workspace = workspace.escape (Escape), workspace.group-selection (G), workspace.edit-set-membership (Ctrl+G), workspace.select-all (Ctrl+A), workspace.copy (Ctrl+C), workspace.cut (Ctrl+X), workspace.paste (Ctrl+V), workspace.undo (Ctrl+Z), workspace.redo (Ctrl+Y; Ctrl+Shift+Z), workspace.delete (Delete), workspace.reveal-selection (Ctrl+Enter), workspace.open-selection (Enter). Copy Prompts = copy-prompts.focus-up (ArrowUp), copy-prompts.focus-down (ArrowDown), copy-prompts.navigate-left (ArrowLeft), copy-prompts.navigate-right (ArrowRight), copy-prompts.open-or-toggle (Enter), copy-prompts.edit-or-rename (F2), copy-prompts.delete (Delete; Backspace), copy-prompts.undo (Ctrl+Z), copy-prompts.redo (Ctrl+Y; Ctrl+Shift+Z), copy-prompts.copy (Ctrl+C), copy-prompts.cut (Ctrl+X), copy-prompts.paste (Ctrl+V), copy-prompts.select-all (Ctrl+A), copy-prompts.escape (Escape). There are 27 explicit definitions, with Workspace/Sets in scope `workspace` and Copy Prompts in scope `copy-prompts`.
State shape: creator overrides live at `state.view.preferences.hotkeys.overrides[actionId]`. A value is a canonical string array; absence means the catalog defaults apply, and `[]` is an explicit Unassigned override. `emptyState()` now provides `view.preferences: {}`; unrelated future preference records survive normalization.
Unknown-id policy: known action ids are canonicalized and malformed known values are dropped; unknown action ids and their raw values are copied through and never participate in effective bindings or conflict checks. Resetting known actions removes only known records; unknown records remain. Resetting the last remaining known override removes the empty `overrides` payload.
Conflict model: capture strings are canonicalized and modifier-only presses are incomplete. A proposed binding conflicts only with another action having the same active scope and effective binding (custom overrides replace, rather than append to, defaults). The same key is therefore allowed across `workspace` and `copy-prompts`; conflict attempts return the conflicting action and leave preferences unchanged.
Files changed so far: public/app/hotkeys-model.js (new pure catalog/persistence/conflict model), public/workspace-model-20260730b.js (normalize and default the project-owned preferences field), hotkeys-model.test.mjs (14 behavioral model regressions), model.test.mjs (updated exact view-shape expectation), package.json (registers the focused suite), and BRAIN-WORKER.txt (pickup/checkpoint log). No controller, page, set-rendering, Papers, or state.json file was changed.
Interpretation: Phase A is green and the catalog/model contract is now stable enough for later consumers. The injected catalog regression exercises registration through the same model path; the Hotkeys UI architectural rendering test and dynamic controller routing remain deliberately deferred to Phase B.
Next bounded step: Stop at the PHASE A CHECKPOINT for BRAIN review as explicitly requested. Do not begin Phase B/C or Phase D in this run.

[2026-08-04 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN REVIEW — Phase D RETURNED — bottom runs render backwards
Phase D is not accepted. The creator's single most specific requirement for this feature — that the
repeated name is never mirrored or upside down — is not met, and the test suite asserts the broken
behavior as correct. Do not proceed to the completion gate. The glyph system, the decorative/
physical separation and the integration are otherwise sound and must NOT be redesigned.

BLOCKING DEFECT 1 — reversed runs spell the title backwards.
public/set-glyph-model.js line 127:
  const characterIndex = reversed ? (chars.length - 1 - (i % chars.length)) : (i % chars.length);
This reverses the GLYPH ORDER only. Placement positions still advance forward along the perimeter
(line 124, distance increases monotonically with i). The assignment requires reversing traversal AND
glyph order TOGETHER, precisely so the two cancel and the word reads left-to-right. With only one of
the two reversed, they do not cancel — they produce a backwards word.

Measured by BRAIN on production geometry (48-point outlines, layoutTitleGlyphs directly):
- title READABILITY, lower half reads: BADAERYTILIBADAERYTILIBADAERYTI  ("YTILIBADAER" = READABILITY
  spelled backwards)
- title PROJECT ALPHA, lower half reads: JORPAHPLA TCEJORPAHPLA TCEJORPAHPLA
- title NARROW on a tall outline, lower half reads: ANWORRANWORRANWORRANWORR
- title SEAM around a full loop: SEAMSEAMSEAMSEAM then MAESMAESMAESMAES then back to SEAM
The upper half is correct. The lower half is mirrored text. This is exactly the failure the
assignment names in its non-negotiable list and exactly what the creator will see first.

BLOCKING DEFECT 2 — the regression asserts the defect.
set-glyph-model.test.mjs line 22:
  assert.deepEqual(bottom.slice(0, 3).map(({ char }) => char), ['C', 'B', 'A']);
For the title 'ABC', the bottom run is asserted to be C, B, A. The test does not merely fail to
catch the defect; it locks it in. Any correct fix will fail this assertion. This is why the phase
reported green. Rewrite the assertion to express the requirement — a reader scanning the bottom run
left-to-right sees the title forward — not the current output.

What I verified as CORRECT and accepted (do not rework):
- Every rendered placement rotation is within the upright half-plane. I checked five outline shapes
  (ellipse, wide, tall, small, long-title): 0 rotations outside +/-90, max |rotation| 89.16 deg. The
  model correctly carries a raw `angle` and a normalized `rotation`, and only `rotation` is rendered
  (line 129 and line 140). No mirroring of glyph coordinates occurs; the normal is derived from the
  normalized tangent.
- The hard decorative/physical invariant HOLDS. layoutTitleGlyphs does not mutate the outline it is
  given; a titled and an untitled run of the same outline are byte-identical; outlineCentroid is
  identical for both. Glyphs render into a separate SVG path element and are never returned as a
  hull or read by physics.
- Layout is deterministic (identical input, identical path), blank/whitespace titles emit zero
  glyphs, and a 1.5px single-vertex deformation moves the first glyph by 0.004px — the top-anchoring
  requirement is genuinely met, no crawl.
- No duplicate/overlapping glyph placements at the closed seam (0 pairs within 0.5px).
- Full suite 632/632; scope contains no Papers or state.json change.

Correction to my own earlier reading, recorded so the worker does not chase it: my first probe
reported 7 angles outside the upright half-plane. That probe collected the raw `angle` field from
intermediate sample points, not rendered output. Re-measured against `rotation` on the actual
placements, the count is 0. There is no orientation defect. Defect 1 is about character ORDER, not
rotation.

SECONDARY — evidence is thin for the phase with the least test-visible failure modes.
Phase D added 5 tests against 11 required regressions. Missing, and each is required: a sufficiently
large ring contains the full title more than once (completeRepeats is asserted only on one square);
a bottom/reversed run spells the word in readable order (currently asserts the opposite); stable top
anchoring under small deformation; closed seam without duplicate/overlapping placement; titled
versus untitled producing equivalent hull/hit-test/exclusion/separation INPUTS AND RESULTS — the
current test checks only that the input array is unmutated, not that a physics consumer agrees;
multiple named sets, Venn overlaps, shared members, retirement and selection rendering without DOM
leaks or stale glyph paths. My probe covers several of these but a probe I delete protects nobody.

PERFORMANCE — investigate before completion, do not tune blindly.
The reported median is 2.9052ms for 12 named sets, which is 17.4% of a 16.7ms frame budget, with a
measured path output of 260592 bytes. The assignment says to investigate if the work would consume a
material part of a frame. 17% is material. For comparison, the accepted whole set-force at 16 sets
measures 0.461ms. I measured a single 48-point outline with a 13-character title at 30100 path
bytes, so the size scales with glyph count as expected.
Required: establish whether this cost is paid EVERY frame or only when outline/title/scale changes
enough to matter. The assignment explicitly permits caching on those keys and explicitly forbids
caching stale geometry that visibly detaches from a moving ring. Report which it is, with the
recompute trigger named, before claiming Phase D is complete. If it is per-frame at 2.9ms, that is a
real finding and must be reported as such, not smoothed over.

Enter still confirms and Escape still cancels the rename, because those are the rename editor's own
keys, not workspace commands. Do not solve this by removing or rebinding G.

workspace command fires and the field receives the character; with rename inactive, assert G still

typed spaces and got ? glyphs.
Root cause, located by BRAIN: public/set-glyph-model.js line 72 correctly PRESERVES ' ' as a space
character, but line 40 does `const rows = BITMAP[key] ?? BITMAP['?']` and BITMAP has no ' ' entry,
so the space falls through to the unsupported-character fallback at render time.
This is the more serious finding of the three, for a reason that outlives this bug: there is a
passing test asserting "whitespace remains a gap." It passes at the layout layer while the RENDERED
path substitutes ?. The test and the pixels disagree. Any repair must make the regression assert on
the emitted path geometry — that a space contributes NO stroke segments — not merely that the
layout array preserved a ' ' character. A test that cannot see what is drawn is not protecting the
drawing.
Also visible in the screenshot near the top of the same set: ?TO?N??HARGE?STOE?. Investigate
whether that is only the space defect plus the seam, or a second glyph/advance problem. Report what
it actually is; do not assume.

[2026-08-04 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN — Assignment 006 (ACTIVE)
THE LETTERING IS UNREADABLE — REPLACE THE BITMAP WITH A TRUE SINGLE-STROKE ALPHABET
======================================================================

Creator report, from the running app: "the font is unreadable."

This is a genuine defect against the original specification, not a taste adjustment. Assignment 004
required "a small project-owned, single-stroke monospaced glyph description in normalized
coordinates" and "single-stroke, ASCII-art-like vector letters." What was built is a 5x7 BITMAP
converted to strokes, which is a different thing and reads as mush at perimeter scale.

Root cause, located by BRAIN in public/set-glyph-model.js:
- BITMAP (line 7) stores each character as seven 5-bit pixel rows.
- segmentsFor (line 37) converts lit pixels to line segments via addRuns, and calls it TWICE:
  addRuns(false) at line 68 extracts horizontal pixel runs, addRuns(true) at line 69 extracts
  vertical runs over the SAME pixels.
- Every lit pixel is therefore drawn by both a horizontal and a vertical segment. Each letter is a
  dense lattice of short, overlapping, mutually redundant strokes rather than a legible letterform.
At the small glyph height used around a perimeter, adjacent 1/4-unit strokes visually merge into a
smudge. This also explains two accepted-but-disliked measurements: the ~307KB path output and the
3.7ms moving-frame cost. A true single-stroke A is 3 segments; the current bitmap A is roughly 15
overlapping ones.

Required outcome
----------------
Replace the bitmap-derived geometry with a genuine single-stroke (engraving/plotter-style) alphabet:
each glyph is a small ordered list of polyline strokes in normalized 0..1 coordinates, authored as
letterforms, NOT derived from a pixel grid. Think of how a pen draws the letter in one or a few
continuous movements. Coverage must remain at least A-Z, 0-9, space and the punctuation already
present, with the ? fallback retained and space still emitting zero strokes.

Constraints
-----------
- Keep the existing public API of set-glyph-model.js. layoutTitleGlyphs, glyphPath and the placement
  record shape must not change, so no caller, test, or the renderer needs restructuring.
- Do NOT change traversal, reversed-run handling, rotation normalization, top anchoring, seam
  behavior, scaling, or the caching key. Assignments 004 and 005 are accepted; this is a glyph-shape
  replacement only.
- Keep glyphs decorative. No physics consumer, hull, or hit-test may see them. The existing
  equivalence regression must continue to pass unchanged.
- Monospaced advance must be preserved so spacing and repetition stay deterministic.
- No SVG <text>, no browser font, no bitmap, no ImageGen, no external font file or library.

Readability is the acceptance criterion, and it is visual
---------------------------------------------------------
This defect was invisible to every existing test because the tests assert on placements, rotations
and path bytes — none of which can see that a letter is illegible. Do not add a test that merely
asserts a stroke count and call it proven.
Required evidence, in this order:
1. Render phase-d-fixture.html at REALISTIC scale — the glyph height actually used on a normal set
   perimeter, not an enlarged specimen — and inspect it. Include at minimum a multi-word title with
   spaces and a title using the full alphabet and digits.
2. Additionally render a flat specimen line of the complete alphabet and digits at that same scale
   so each letterform can be judged individually.
3. State in the checkpoint what you can actually READ, per character. If any character is ambiguous
   at that size, say which and fix the letterform rather than reporting it as acceptable.
4. Report the new stroke count per glyph and the new total path bytes against the current ~307KB,
   and re-measure the moving-frame cost against the current 3.74ms median. A real single-stroke
   alphabet should reduce all three substantially; if it does not, something is wrong.

Regressions to keep or add
--------------------------
- Every Assignment 004 and 005 glyph regression must still pass, including readable reversed runs,
  spaces emitting zero geometry, ? fallback, upright rotations, anchoring and seam distinctness.
- Add a regression asserting no glyph emits duplicate or exactly-overlapping segments, which is the
  specific structural defect being removed here and would catch a regression to the doubled-run form.
- Mutation-check that rule: reintroduce the second addRuns pass (or an equivalent duplicate-stroke
  source), assert the mutation is on disk by line index, confirm the new regression fails, restore.
  Carried warning: string replacement against these CRLF files silently failed for BRAIN repeatedly
  this session.

Out of scope
------------
No physics, forces, hull, exclusion, separation, catalog, hotkeys page, persistence, colour, context
menu or rename work. No commit, push, branch change, build, install, launch or restart. No anime.js
or any third-party library — the creator raised it as a possible future direction and BRAIN has not
authorized a dependency; do not introduce one under this assignment.

Append a WORKER PICKUP, then execute. Append a WORKER COMPLETION — Assignment 006 and stop for BRAIN
review. The creator's eye is the acceptance test for this one; do not claim readability without
having looked at the render yourself and said what you saw.

[2026-08-04 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN — Assignment 007 (ACTIVE)
DECORATIVE MOTION: DRAG TRAILS AND PER-SET SELECTION EFFECTS
======================================================================

Baseline: feat/sets-ring @ 7d04f87, clean tree, 639/639 passing.

The creator asked for two effects and named anime.js as the library they want used:
1. Trailing effects while dragging items around.
2. Overlapping effects on multiple selected sets, a random effect per set — "could be pulsing
   waves, could be anything".

Dependency decision (BRAIN)
---------------------------
anime.js v4 is AUTHORIZED, vendored only. This is not an architecture change: public/vendor/ already
holds d3-force.js, d3-selection.js and d3-zoom.js as pre-bundled ES modules imported by relative
path. anime.js follows that exact pattern.
- Obtain the ESM build and place it at public/vendor/anime.js. Import by relative path like the d3
  modules. Record the exact version in package.json dependencies for provenance, matching how
  d3-force is recorded.
- NO bundler, NO build step, NO import map, NO CDN. The entry HTML sets a strict CSP
  (script-src 'self'; connect-src 'none'), so a remote script would be blocked outright and a build
  step would end the live-edit workflow this project depends on.
- Nothing else may be added. One dependency, this assignment.

THE HARD RULE — decoration never writes to the simulation
----------------------------------------------------------
This is the invariant the whole assignment hangs on, and it is not negotiable.
Effects READ positions and geometry. They never WRITE to anything d3-force owns — not x, y, vx, vy,
fx, fy, not the ring nodes, not shape.outline, and not any value a force reads. An effect that
animates a simulation-owned property becomes a second writer, and this branch's own history records
what that produces: a settled scene that pumped itself apart to 7495px because one force kept
injecting velocity while everything else cooled.
Concretely: animate presentation-only attributes on separate decorative SVG elements. Do not attach
anime.js to item nodes, ring nodes, the set body path, or the glyph path whose d attribute is
regenerated each frame. Decorative elements live in their own layer, are pointer-events none, and
are never consulted by hit-testing, containment, exclusion, separation, gravity, or nesting order.

Feature 1 — drag trails
------------------------
Creator-visible outcome: dragging an item leaves a brief visual trail that fades behind it.
Why this one is the riskier half, stated so the worker does not treat it casually:
- A drag is the busiest frame in the app. pointer-controller pins fx/fy AND x/y per step and reheats
  to 0.12; the simulation, ring reconciliation and hull recomputation all run. If the dragged item
  belongs to a named set, the outline changes every frame, which invalidates the glyph cache and
  regenerates the lettering (about 1.35ms). A trail adds work exactly where there is least headroom.
- This repository's history is specifically about drag reproduction going wrong. Three harnesses
  modelled a drag as one large jump instead of many small steps that pin fx/fy and x/y and reheat;
  one produced a diagnosis so wrong it cost a branch retirement. Any trail test MUST use the real
  many-small-steps gesture shape.
Requirements:
- The trail samples the dragged item's position per frame and renders decorative marks. It must not
  alter drag behavior: pointer tracking error must remain exactly 0, as the Assignment 002 harness
  already measures.
- The trail must clear completely on release, and must not leave stale nodes if a drag is cancelled,
  the item is deleted mid-drag, or the view navigates away.
- Multi-item drags must not multiply cost without bound. State the chosen behavior (per item, or one
  trail for the selection) and why.
- Measure the added per-frame cost during an actual drag, warmed up, median and range, against the
  1.35ms lettering and the existing force cost. If the trail pushes a dragging frame near the 16.7ms
  budget, report it as a finding rather than shipping it quietly.

Feature 2 — per-set selection effects
--------------------------------------
Creator-visible outcome: selecting sets plays a decorative effect on each; multiple selected sets
show effects simultaneously and may overlap; each set gets a random effect.
Creator decision, recorded and to be implemented AS ASKED: the effect is RE-ROLLED on every
selection. BRAIN recommended stable-per-set (same set always gets the same effect, so it reads as
character rather than flicker); the creator chose re-roll. Build re-roll.
- Because re-roll was chosen, pay attention to the failure mode it invites: rapid re-selection must
  not stack effects, leak timelines, or leave a set mid-animation when it is deselected. Every
  effect must be cancellable and must clean up its own DOM and timeline on deselect, retirement,
  and re-roll.
- Implement at least three visually distinct effects (pulsing waves are the creator's example; the
  others are the worker's choice within the warm-paper visual language). Effects use the set's own
  hue and must not override the accepted selected/picking/chosen/partial emphasis.
- Effects run on the SETTLED drawn outline. They are a separate decorative layer, not the body path
  and not the glyph path.
- Overlapping sets must both animate without one clipping or hiding the other's body or lettering.

Testing and evidence
--------------------
- Behavioral regressions: a drag with trails keeps pointer error at 0 and the physics output
  unchanged; trails clear on release/cancel/delete; selection starts an effect and deselection stops
  and cleans it; rapid re-selection does not accumulate timelines or DOM nodes; retirement removes
  effects; hit-testing and containment results are identical with effects on and off.
- The decorative/physical equivalence regression must be extended to cover effects, proving physics
  output is byte-identical with animation active and inactive.
- Mutation-check the hard rule: make an effect write to a simulation-owned property, assert the
  mutation is on disk BY LINE INDEX, and confirm the equivalence regression fails. Carried warning:
  string replacement against these CRLF files silently failed for BRAIN repeatedly this session.
- Render a fixture showing the effects and inspect it yourself; state what you actually saw. A
  fixture that is generated and never opened has already missed a defect once on this branch.
- Full npm test with old/new counts, node --check, git diff --check, NUL scan.

Out of scope
------------
No physics, forces, constants, hull, exclusion, separation, gravity or ring changes. No hotkeys,
catalog, persistence, colour-solver, rename or context-menu changes. No glyph geometry changes —
the period/comma faintness is logged and is NOT this assignment's work. No second dependency. No
commit, push, merge, PR, branch change, release, install, build, launch or restart; the previous
push authorization was single-use and has been consumed.

already ignores, so it establishes the effect layer and its lifecycle safely. Feature 1 (drag

[2026-08-04 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN — Assignment 008 (ACTIVE)
INTERSECTION REGIONS AS THEIR OWN COLOUR PATCHES
======================================================================

Baseline: feat/sets-ring @ 7d04f87 plus the accepted, uncommitted Assignment 007 work. 648/648.

Creator report: where two sets overlap, the lens is currently the blend of the two translucent
fills, which "looks darker and less natural". The creator wants each intersection treated as a patch
of colour in its own right, following the same colour function as everything else.

Root cause, located by BRAIN: public/styles/graph.css line 34 draws each set as
`fill: color-mix(in srgb, var(--set-color) 12%, transparent)`. Two overlapping translucent fills
composite to roughly 23% alpha, which is darker and muddier than either parent. Nothing is deciding
the lens colour; it is a compositing artefact.

Creator decisions, recorded and to be implemented as asked:
1. LENS COLOUR: each intersection region is fed into the SAME spatial hue solver
   (assignSpatialFolderHues) as its own entity, positioned at the region's centre, so it receives a
   hue separated from both parents exactly as a third folder would. Not derived from its parents.
2. DEPTH: every distinct region formed by any combination of overlapping sets becomes its own patch,
   not pairs only. Three-way and deeper overlaps are included.

Required outcome
----------------
An overlap reads as its own flat patch of colour at the same visual strength as an ordinary set
body, rather than as a darker stack. Each distinct region — the parts belonging to one set alone,
and each intersection of any combination — is one patch with one solved hue.

THE HARD RULE — this is decoration and must not reach physics
--------------------------------------------------------------
This assignment is the closest anything has come to the accepted physics since Assignment 003, so
state the boundary precisely.
- The regions computed here are a PRESENTATION decomposition. They must never be returned as a hull,
  supplied to hullOf, or read by containment, hit-testing, ejection, exclusion, separation, gravity,
  ring reconciliation, or nesting order.
- shape.outline remains the single physical geometry every physics consumer reads, unchanged.
- Set membership, the shared-member Venn exemption, and the exclusion/separation behaviour accepted
  in Assignments 001-003 are untouched. The Venn lens must still FORM exactly as it does today; only
  its fill colour changes.
- Hit-testing must still select the smallest containing set as it does now. A creator clicking
  inside a lens must get the same set they get today, not a region.
Mutation-check this: make a region leak into a physics consumer, assert the mutation on disk BY LINE
INDEX, and confirm a physics-equivalence regression fails.

Implementation guidance, not prescription
------------------------------------------
- The regions are intersections and differences of convex outlines. The repository already has
  convex geometry helpers in set-ring-model.js from the accepted work; prefer reusing proven
  primitives over inventing a new clipper. Convex-convex intersection is well-defined and cheap.
- Note graph.css already sets `fill-rule: evenodd` on the set outline; check whether the existing
  path construction interacts with the new decomposition before assuming a clean slate.
- Region identity must be STABLE and namespace-safe in the hue map. Two sets overlapping should
  produce the same region id regardless of which set is drawn first or the order ids arrive in;
  otherwise the lens hue will flicker as the solver re-runs. Derive the id from the sorted set ids.
  Folders use `folder:`, sets use `set:`; pick a third distinct prefix.
- Regions appear and vanish as sets move. Hue assignment must handle a region appearing mid-session
  without a jarring jump, matching the folder warm-start behaviour already in the solver. Do not
  persist region colour as creator data.
- Cost grows with overlapping-set count. Measure it. Recompute only when the outlines actually
  change, in the spirit of the accepted glyph cache key, and do not cache stale region geometry that
  visibly detaches from a moving set.

Required regressions
--------------------
- A two-set overlap produces exactly three regions (A-only, B-only, A-and-B) with three distinct
  solved hues, and the lens hue is not the composite of its parents.
- A three-set overlap produces every distinct region, including the centre region.
- Disjoint sets produce one region each and render exactly as they do today.
- Region ids are order-independent: the same overlap yields the same id regardless of input order.
- Nearby regions respect the same hard hue-separation invariant folders already have.
- Physics equivalence: hull, containment, hit-test, exclusion and separation results are identical
  with region rendering active and inactive. This is the load-bearing one.
- Hit-testing still returns the smallest containing SET, not a region.
- Moving a set updates its regions smoothly; a set retiring removes its regions with no stale paths.

Evidence
--------
Render a fixture with two overlapping sets AND a three-way overlap, inspect it yourself, and state
what you actually saw — including whether the lens still reads as a distinct region rather than a
muddy stack. A fixture generated and never opened has missed a real defect on this branch before.
Full npm test with old/new counts, node --check, git diff --check, NUL scan.

Out of scope
------------
No physics, forces, constants, hull geometry, exclusion, separation, gravity or ring changes. No
membership or Venn-exemption changes. No glyph, hotkey, catalog, persistence, rename or context-menu
work. No new dependency — anime.js is already vendored and nothing further is authorized. No commit,
push, merge, PR, branch change, release, install, build, launch or restart.

Append a WORKER PICKUP, then execute. Append a WORKER COMPLETION — Assignment 008 and stop.
[2026-08-04 14:55 Asia/Saigon] WORKER PICKUP — Assignment 008

Cover two-set, three-set, disjoint, order-independent ids, hue separation, moving/retiring regions, physics equivalence, render inspection, and cost evidence.

SET SELECTION EFFECTS MUST FILL THE INTERIOR, NOT DECORATE THE BORDER
======================================================================

Creator feedback on the Assignment 007 effects, verbatim: "I dont want shits on the border, I want it

timelines, and full cleanup on deselect/re-roll/retirement/clear all stay. Only what is drawn changes.

Why the current effects miss the intent, so the worker does not rebuild the same thing:
All three variants (wave, orbit, spark) are dashed strokes travelling along the set's perimeter,
differing only in dasharray, width and duration. They deliver variety of TEXTURE, not variety of
IDEA, and they rest at 0.04-0.12 opacity for most of their cycle so they barely register. The creator
asked for "could be pulsing waves, could be anything" and received three dash patterns on a border.
Worse, a NAMED set has no ordinary border stroke at all — its letters are the border — so a border
effect runs along an invisible line. That interaction was never specified and reads as wrong.

Required outcome
----------------
A selected set is unmistakable at a glance because its INTERIOR animates. The effect is a fill
treatment on the set's area, not a stroke on its edge.
- Effects must be structurally DIFFERENT ideas, not one idea with different parameters. Examples the
  creator named or implied: a pulse that breathes the whole interior, ripples radiating outward from
  the centre, a slow wash or gradient sweep across the area. At least three, each recognisably its
  own thing rather than a re-parameterised sibling.
- Strength: the creator must be able to tell a selected set from an unselected one instantly, without
  looking for it. The current 0.04-0.12 resting opacity is the specific failure; do not repeat it.
- The effect must remain readable UNDER the set's name lettering and must not make the letters harder
  to read. Named sets have no border stroke, so the interior is the only surface available.

Interaction with Assignment 008 — this is the important part
--------------------------------------------------------------
Assignment 008 gives every distinct region its own solved hue and its own patch in
`.graph-set-region-layer`, and moves the set body's translucent fill onto those region patches
(`.graph-set-region`, currently 12% and 24% selected). That region layer is the correct surface for
an interior effect, and it did not exist when Assignment 007 was written.
- Build the interior effects on the region surface, not on the set body path.
- Do NOT let an effect destroy the region colour distinction 008 exists to create. A selected set's
  regions must still read as their own hues while animating. Modulate the existing region fill;
  do not paint over it with one flat animated colour.
- An overlap region belongs to more than one set. Decide and STATE what happens when one parent set
  is selected and the other is not, and when both are selected with different effects. Do not let two
  effects fight over the same patch. If the answer is not obvious from 008's structure once it lands,
  append a WORKER QUESTION rather than guessing.
- 008 must be BRAIN-accepted before this assignment starts. Do not build against a moving target.

[2026-08-04 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN — Assignment 011 (ACTIVE, TAKES PRECEDENCE)
PULSE IS A PHOTOSENSITIVITY HAZARD — REMOVE IT NOW; RIPPLE DOES NOT SCALE
======================================================================

Creator eye check of Assignment 009: "pulse is seizure inducing, cant see the ripple, only wash
confirmed."

PRECEDENCE: finish or suspend the in-flight Assignment 010 work cleanly, then do this FIRST. Do not
leave the tree in a half-edited state, but do not carry on adding Settings features while a
photosensitivity hazard sits in the working tree. If 010 edits are already substantial and safely
green, it is acceptable to complete them and immediately start this; state which you chose.

DEFECT 1 — pulse (SAFETY, not taste)
-------------------------------------
public/set-effects-model.js line 82: `fillOpacity: [0.55, 1, 0.55]` over 1100ms, looping. That is a
near-doubling of a LARGE AREA's opacity roughly once per second — full-amplitude, whole-body
flashing, unbounded by set size. The creator experienced it as seizure inducing and that report is
authoritative; do not treat it as a preference to be tuned by taste.
Required: REMOVE the pulse effect entirely. Same removal discipline as the border effects — gone from
the model, gone from CSS, its assertions removed or rewritten, no flag, no fallback, no dead class.
Do NOT replace it with a gentler flash. The creator has now rejected two border effects and one
flashing effect; the lesson is that whole-area luminance oscillation is the wrong instrument here.
General rule to apply to every current and future effect in this project, and to record in the
completion: no effect may oscillate the opacity, fill or luminance of a large area at a rate the eye
reads as flashing. Slow, low-amplitude, or spatially-localised motion only. If an effect's amplitude
times its area times its frequency is what makes it noticeable, it is the wrong effect.

DEFECT 2 — ripple is invisible because it does not scale
---------------------------------------------------------
public/set-effects-model.js line 104: `r: [3, 28]`. A fixed 28px maximum radius, from the region
centre, regardless of region size. Sets on this canvas are hundreds of pixels across, so the ripple
is a speck near the middle. It is not too faint — it is too SMALL.
The module already has `bboxFromPath` (line 42), which returns the region's real bounding box, and
`wash` uses it. Ripple does not. That is the whole bug, and it is also exactly why wash is the only
effect the creator can see: wash is the only one that measures the region it draws into.
Required: scale the ripple to the region. Derive its maximum radius from the region's own bbox so a
large set gets a large ripple and a small set a proportionate one. Keep it spatially localised and
low-amplitude — an expanding ring reads as motion without flashing, which is the point.
Add a regression asserting the ripple's radius scales with region size: a region several times larger
must produce a proportionately larger maximum radius. A fixed-radius implementation must fail it.

WHAT SURVIVES
-------------
- wash is CREATOR-CONFIRMED. Do not modify it. It is now the reference for what a good effect looks
  like here: region-measured, gradual, spatially varying rather than globally flashing.
- The accepted lifecycle contract stands unchanged: pointer-events-none layer, one deterministic
  owner per shared region, re-roll on new selection, cancellable timelines, full cleanup on
  deselect/re-roll/retirement/clear, and fill-opacity restored to null so region colours are never
  left corrupted. The 50-rapid-re-selection no-leak property must still hold.
- With pulse removed, decide and STATE how many effects remain in the re-roll pool. Two (wash and a
  fixed ripple) is acceptable. If a third is added it must be a genuinely different idea AND obey the
  no-flashing rule above; do not invent one merely to restore the count to three.

EVIDENCE
--------
- Render a fixture at REALISTIC set sizes — several hundred pixels across, not a small specimen —
  showing each surviving effect on a named set and on an overlapping pair. Inspect it yourself and
  state what you actually saw, including whether the ripple is visible at that size and whether the
  lettering stays readable. Ripple's invisibility would have been caught by a realistic-size fixture;
  a small one hid it.
- Confirm no remaining effect flashes: state each surviving effect's amplitude, area and frequency.
- Mutation-check the ripple scaling rule: fix the radius back to a constant, assert the mutation on
  disk BY LINE INDEX, confirm the new scaling regression fails, restore.
- Full npm test with old/new counts, node --check, git diff --check, NUL scan.

[2026-08-04 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN — Assignment 013 (QUEUED — after 012)
A SET MUST NOT DRAW INSIDE A FOLDER IT MERELY CONTAINS
======================================================================

Creator report with a screenshot taken at breadcrumb `As you Go > Feed`: a set named LETTERS is drawn
around the items inside Feed. LETTERS belongs to a different level. Creator's rule, verbatim: "sets
from one level shouldnt appear anywhere else."

Cause, located by BRAIN and to be confirmed by the worker before changing anything:
public/sets-model.js belongsToSet (line 125) walks the item's ANCESTOR CHAIN —
`[itemId, ...ancestorsOf(itemId)]` — and returns true if any ancestor is a member. So when the Feed
folder (or an ancestor of Feed) is a member of LETTERS, every item inside Feed inherits that
membership. Navigating into Feed makes those items visible, memberRects
(workspace-20260730b.js:618) collects them, and the ring draws around them one level down.
That inheritance is DELIBERATE and ACCEPTED — it is what makes "add a folder to a set" work, and it
is load-bearing for containment, exclusion and the Venn exemption. The defect is not the inheritance
itself; it is that the OUTLINE follows the creator into the member folder, where the set has no
meaning as a visible grouping.

Creator decision, recorded: draw a set only at the level it is owned at — the level whose items are
its own members. Navigating into a member folder must not bring the set's outline in with you.

THE HARD BOUNDARY — do not change membership semantics
-------------------------------------------------------
This is a RENDERING/VISIBILITY change, not a membership change. State this back in the pickup.
- belongsToSet, setsContaining, inheritance through folders, excludedIds, and the shared-member Venn
  exemption keep their exact current behaviour. Do not edit sets-model.js membership rules to fix a
  drawing problem.
- Nothing about which items a set CONTAINS changes. Only which sets are DRAWN in the current view.
- Accepted physics stays closed: forces, hull, exclusion, separation, gravity, ring reconciliation
  and their constants are untouched. A set that is not drawn simply has no ring in this view; it must
  not leave orphaned ring nodes, half-retired shapes, stale glyph paths or leaked effect timelines.
- The existing retirement path already fades a set whose members leave the screen. Prefer reusing it
  over inventing a second disappearance mechanism, and make sure navigating in/out does not read as
  a set "popping".

Implementation guidance
------------------------
`session.currentId` is the current folder and is already available where sets are drawn
(workspace-20260730b.js:1489 passes it to visibleGraphItems). The natural seam is deciding which sets
are eligible to draw for the current navigation level, before rings are reconciled — not filtering
member rectangles after the fact, which would leave a set with a partial or empty ring.
Define "owned at this level" precisely and write the definition into the completion: a set is drawn
in the current view when it has at least one DIRECT member (an id in its own memberIds, not an
inherited descendant) among the items visible at this level. Confirm that definition against the
creator's screenshot before building on it — LETTERS must stop drawing inside Feed, and must still
draw at the level where its own members live.

Edge cases that must be decided and stated, not discovered later
-----------------------------------------------------------------
- A set whose direct members span TWO folders: it draws in each folder where a direct member is
  visible. Confirm that is coherent with the creator's rule and say so.
- Expanded folders: the graph can show a folder's contents inline via graphExpanded without
  navigating into it. Decide whether an inline-expanded folder counts as "this level" and justify the
  choice; the creator's complaint is about NAVIGATION, so inline expansion may reasonably differ.
- The Bin view and its separate navigation must not start drawing sets that were never drawn there.
- A set with no visible direct members at any level must not linger as an invisible ring.

Required regressions
--------------------
- A set owning a folder does NOT draw inside that folder after navigating into it, while the folder's
  items still BELONG to the set (belongsToSet unchanged) — this is the load-bearing pair and both
  halves must be asserted together.
- The same set still draws at the level where its direct members live.
- A set with direct members in two folders draws in both.
- Navigating in and back out restores the set cleanly: no duplicate rings, no stale glyph path, no
  leaked effect timeline, no orphaned region patch.
- Containment, hit-testing, exclusion and separation results are unchanged for items that remain
  visible — the physics-equivalence property must still hold.
- Mutation-check the new eligibility rule: revert it to the inherited-membership behaviour, assert
  the mutation on disk BY LINE INDEX, and confirm the first regression fails for that reason.

Evidence
--------
Reproduce the creator's exact scenario first (a folder that is a member of a set, navigate into it)
and show it failing before the fix. Render a fixture or harness output showing the set present at its
own level and absent one level down, and state what you actually saw. Full npm test with old/new
counts, node --check, git diff --check, NUL scan.

Out of scope
------------
No membership-model changes, no physics, no glyph/effect/trail/region work, no hotkeys or Settings

[2026-08-04 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN — SECOND photosensitivity report; hue easing slowed 5x (done by BRAIN)
Creator report: the set colours are the second eye-strain problem. "since its much larger than a
folders outline, the abrupt colour change strains my eye. make the function the same, but the colour
change about five times slower, meaning no cycling over 20 colours in 1 sec."

Change made directly by BRAIN, disclosed here so the worker does not mistake it for its own work:
public/graph-model-20260730b.js BASE_MOVE 0.15 -> 0.03 (line ~213). That constant is the bounded
fraction each hue moves toward its position-derived base per call; it is the single rate governing
the whole colour chase, so the creator's "same function, five times slower" maps onto it exactly.
No other constant, and no part of the solver's logic, was touched — MIN_HUE_SEPARATION, the
projection passes, the warm start and the position derivation are all unchanged.

Measured before/after on an isolated 90-degree swing (assignSpatialFolderHues driven directly):
after 1 frame 87.3deg remaining; 60 frames 14.5deg; 120 frames 2.3deg; settles under 1deg at 148
frames, about 2.5s at 60fps. Previously the same swing settled in roughly a fifth of that, which is
the "20 colours in 1 sec" the creator described.

One test needed updating and I want the reason on the record, because updating a test to make a
failure disappear is normally forbidden: renderer.test.mjs 'pulls an isolated folder toward its new
position base' asserted convergence within a 60-FRAME window. That window encoded the OLD RATE, not
the behaviour. The test's real claims — the hue moves toward its new base, and it converges onto it —
both still hold; only the timing changed, by design. I widened the window to 300 frames and wrote the
reason into the test as a comment.
I then verified I had not simply weakened the check: with BASE_MOVE mutated to 0 (hue never moves),
asserted on disk by line index, THREE tests fail including this one. The widened window still catches
a dead solver. Restored; 661/661 green; node --check clean.
Process note for the worker: my first attempt at that mutation silently did not apply because the
shell had reset to a parent directory, and the run reported a meaningless 47/47 pass. This is the
same class of failure the handoff warns about. Always confirm a mutation is on disk before believing
a result — I use absolute paths for this reason.

STANDING SAFETY RULE — WIDENED, supersedes the narrower version in Assignment 011
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The Assignment 011 rule covered opacity/fill/luminance oscillation. That was too narrow: HUE is a
second flashing channel and it is how this defect reached the creator. The rule now reads:

No visual behaviour in this project may change a large area's appearance — opacity, fill, luminance,
OR hue — at a rate the eye reads as flashing or strobing. Amplitude x area x frequency is the hazard,
and a set body is a large area. Prefer slow, low-amplitude, or spatially-localised motion. When in
doubt, slower and smaller.

Apply this to anything that animates or eases, not only to the named "effects": easing constants,
transitions, retirement fades, selection emphasis, and any future addition. The creator has now
reported eye strain TWICE — once from opacity flashing (pulse) and once from hue cycling. Both
reached them because a value was chosen without reference to the area it would cover. Before shipping
any animated or eased value, state what area it affects and at what rate.

NOT creator-verified: the slowed hue easing has not been seen in the running app. The creator should
confirm the colour drift now reads as gentle rather than strained.

[2026-08-04 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN — Assignment 015 (ACTIVE, TAKES PRECEDENCE — third eye-strain report)
POSITION MAPS TO HUE TOO VIOLENTLY; THE CENTRE IS A 180-DEGREE DISCONTINUITY
======================================================================

Creator report, after the BASE_MOVE 0.15 -> 0.03 change: "though it is gentler, the absolute position
still have a strong position over the colours change, so it still change rapidly."

The creator is right and my earlier fix addressed the wrong half. BASE_MOVE controls how fast a hue
CHASES its target. It does nothing about how violently the TARGET itself moves. If the target swings
180 degrees, a slower chase just means a slower sweep through every intermediate colour.

Root cause, measured by BRAIN against public/graph-model-20260730b.js positionBase (line 245):
  angle = atan2(y - cy, x - cx) in degrees; base = angle + (radius / RADIUS_SPAN) * RADIUS_WEIGHT
This is a raw 1:1 mapping from canvas angle to hue, and it has two bad properties. Measured numbers,
canvas centre at origin:

(a) THE CENTRE IS A DISCONTINUITY. Crossing it inverts the hue by exactly 180 degrees regardless of
    how small the movement is:
      -40px -> +40px across centre: 180.0 deg
      -20px -> +20px across centre: 180.0 deg
      -10px -> +10px across centre: 180.0 deg
       -2px ->  +2px across centre: 180.0 deg
    A two-pixel nudge flips a set to its opposite colour. This is not a steep gradient, it is a
    singularity, and atan2 has one at the origin by definition.

(b) HYPERSENSITIVITY NEAR THE CENTRE. The same 40px sideways movement produces wildly different hue
    changes depending on distance from centre:
      r=30px  -> 57.9 deg      r=250px -> 9.9 deg
      r=60px  -> 36.6 deg      r=500px -> 5.0 deg
      r=120px -> 20.0 deg      r=800px -> 3.1 deg
    Nineteen times the colour change for an identical gesture, purely because of where the set
    happens to sit. Items cluster near the centre, so this is the common case, not an edge case.

Additionally a full orbit at r=300 sweeps the entire wheel (72, 117, 162, 207, 252, 297, 342, 27),
and the radius term adds a further 120 degrees per 500px travelled outward. A set moving normally
therefore traverses large arcs of the colour wheel as an ordinary consequence of being dragged.

Required outcome
----------------
Position still influences hue — that behaviour is deliberate and the creator has not asked for it to
go away — but ordinary movement must produce a gentle, proportionate colour change, with NO
discontinuity anywhere on the canvas and no hypersensitive region.

Design constraints, not an implementation
------------------------------------------
The worker owns the mechanism. Whatever is chosen must satisfy all of these, and each must be proven
by a regression, not asserted:
1. NO DISCONTINUITY. There is no point on the canvas where an arbitrarily small movement produces a
   large hue change. Specifically, crossing or passing near the centre must not invert the hue.
   Assert this with a sampled sweep across and around the centre, not a single probe pair.
2. BOUNDED SENSITIVITY. There is a stated maximum degrees-of-hue per pixel of movement, and it holds
   everywhere on the canvas including near the centre. State the number. A reasonable target is that
   a normal drag across the workspace changes the hue by a modest fraction of the wheel rather than
   sweeping it.
3. ROUGHLY UNIFORM. The same gesture produces a comparable hue change wherever it happens. The
   current 19x variation between r=30 and r=800 is the defect; state the new worst-case ratio.
4. STILL POSITION-DERIVED AND DETERMINISTIC. Same position, same hue. No randomness, no time
   dependence, no hysteresis. Two sets in the same place still resolve via the existing separation
   invariant.
5. STILL SPANS THE SPECTRUM. Distant folders/sets across the canvas should still land on genuinely
   different hues; do not fix the sensitivity by collapsing everything into a narrow band of
   near-identical colours. State the range actually achieved across a realistic canvas extent.
A smooth low-frequency function of (x, y) — for instance a slowly-varying field rather than a polar
angle — satisfies 1-3 naturally, because the singularity is a property of using the angle at all.
That is a hint, not a mandate; any mechanism meeting the constraints is acceptable.

Preserved behaviour
-------------------
- MIN_HUE_SEPARATION (45 deg) and the nearby-pair projection invariant are ACCEPTED and unchanged.
  Nearby folders and sets must still be pushed apart in hue. Do not weaken the separation to reduce
  sensitivity.
- BASE_MOVE stays at 0.03. It is now correct for what it does; do not compensate for the new
  mapping by changing it as well, or the two changes become impossible to evaluate separately.
- The warm start, the folder/set namespacing from Assignment 008, and the session-only (never
  persisted) nature of hue state all stay as they are.
- Existing renderer.test.mjs hue tests must keep passing where they express behaviour. Where a test
  pins the OLD MAPPING's specific numbers (for example the 72-degree east base), update the
  expectation to express the intent and say in the completion exactly which assertions changed and
  why. Do not delete a check to make a failure disappear.

Standing safety rule applies
-----------------------------
This is the creator's THIRD eye-strain report. The rule recorded above now governs hue as well as
opacity: no large area may change appearance at a rate the eye reads as flashing. A set body is a
large area and hue is a flashing channel. Before shipping, state the worst-case hue change per
second for a realistic drag and confirm it is gentle.

Evidence
--------
- Reproduce the measured numbers above against the CURRENT code first, so the before/after is on the
  record from the worker's own run, not only from mine.
- Report the same table after the change: 40px move at r=30/60/120/250/500/800, the centre-crossing
  sweep, an orbit, and the achieved spectrum range.
- Mutation-check the no-discontinuity rule: reintroduce a raw angular mapping, assert the mutation on
  disk BY LINE INDEX, confirm the discontinuity regression fails, restore.
- Full npm test with old/new counts, node --check, git diff --check, NUL scan.

Out of scope
------------
No physics, membership, effects, trails, glyph or Settings work. No new dependency. No commit, push,
merge, PR, branch change, release, install, build, launch or restart.

PRECEDENCE: this is a creator comfort/accessibility defect and outranks Assignments 012, 013 and 014.
Finish or cleanly suspend whatever is in flight, then do this first. Append a WORKER PICKUP, then
execute. Append a WORKER COMPLETION — Assignment 015 and stop.

Claim being tested: the current raw angular position mapping causes the creator's eye-strain defect.

- Fresh isolated hue samples cross the centre from 180.48° at (-2,0) to 0.48° at (2,0), a 180°
  jump across a 4px sweep. The broader samples were -40=>189.6, -20=>184.8, -10=>182.4,

- A realistic sample grid from x=-800..800 and y=-500..500 produced hues from 0.0° to 282.335°.

atan2's centre singularity and adds a radial term; BASE_MOVE only slows the chase after the target
has already jumped. The accepted near-pair projection will remain unchanged. A low-frequency
Cartesian field with a fixed maximum gradient is the smallest coherent correction.

[2026-08-04 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN — Assignment 016 (ACTIVE, TAKES PRECEDENCE)
SPLIT THE HUE MAPPING: RESTORE FOLDERS EXACTLY AS THEY WERE, KEEP SETS GENTLE
======================================================================

Creator report after Assignment 015: "now everything stays mostly greenishly blueishly monotone",
followed by the decisive correction: "i didmt want this to affect the folders colour, its fine as
is" — then "as WAS". The creator wants FOLDER colours restored to their ORIGINAL pre-015 behaviour,
not the current flattened field.

This is a regression introduced by 015 and it takes precedence over Assignments 012, 013 and 014.

Measured by BRAIN against the current code (HUE_FIELD_ORIGIN 180, X_RATE 0.08, Y_RATE 0.06):
- The creator's three visible sets sit at hues 202.0, 212.8 and 227.0 degrees — a maximum separation
  of 25 degrees, BELOW the MIN_HUE_SEPARATION of 45. All three land in the same blue-green band,
  which is exactly what the creator sees.
- Two entities must be 563px apart in x (or 750px in y) merely to differ by 45 degrees.
- Traversing the full colour wheel takes 4500px in x / 6000px in y — far larger than the canvas.
- Corner-to-corner spread on a 600x500 view is only 78 degrees; on a 1200x800 window, 144 degrees.
The field is far too flat. Assignment 015 satisfied constraints 1-4 (no singularity, bounded, uniform,
deterministic) and FAILED constraint 5 (still spans the spectrum). My constraint 5 was written to
prevent exactly this and it was not met; the failure is in the delivered work, but note for future
assignments that constraint 5 needed a NUMBER, not a description.

ROOT CAUSE OF THE CONFLICT, and the shape of the fix
------------------------------------------------------
One mapping is being asked to serve two things with genuinely opposed requirements:
- FOLDER hues colour a small outline and a small icon tint. Vivid, well-separated colour is good
  there, and the original angular mapping delivered it. The creator has never complained about folder
  colours and has now explicitly said they were fine.
- SET hues fill a LARGE area. That is where rapid or high-contrast change caused eye strain, and it
  is the only reason any of this changed.
Therefore: SPLIT THE MAPPING. Folders keep their original behaviour; sets get the gentle treatment.
Do not keep trying to find one curve that serves both — that attempt is what produced the monotone.

Required outcome
----------------
1. FOLDERS: restore the ORIGINAL pre-Assignment-015 behaviour exactly.
   - The original positionBase was the angular mapping:
       angle = atan2(y - cy, x - cx) in degrees
       base  = wrapDeg(angle + (radius / RADIUS_SPAN) * RADIUS_WEIGHT)
     Recover the exact original from git (HEAD 7d04f87 predates all of this) rather than
     reconstructing it from memory, and confirm byte-level equivalence of the restored function.
   - Folder easing returns to the ORIGINAL BASE_MOVE of 0.15. My 0.15 -> 0.03 change was made for the
     sets' benefit and the creator has said folders were fine as they were.
   - The centre singularity and the near-centre hypersensitivity I measured in Assignment 015 are
     ACCEPTED for folders. They never troubled the creator on a small outline. Do not "improve" them.
2. SETS: keep a gentle mapping, but fix the monotone.

The old check was belongsToSet(itemSet, nodeId, ancestorsOfNode), which honours inheritance. It was
replaced with a DIRECT-ONLY test, so the ring is now built from direct members alone and every
inherited child is excluded from the shape the set draws around.

Assignment 013 asked for direct membership as the test for WHETHER A SET IS ELIGIBLE TO DRAW AT THE
CURRENT LEVEL. It did not ask for it to become the test for WHICH MEMBERS THE RING ENCLOSES. Those
are two different questions and my assignment did not separate them explicitly enough. Recording that

THE TWO QUESTIONS, stated separately and definitively
-------------------------------------------------------
1. ELIGIBILITY — "should this set draw here at all?"
   Uses DIRECT membership. A set draws in the current view only if at least one id in its own
   memberIds is among the visible items. This is the Assignment 013 behaviour the creator asked for
   ("sets from one level shouldnt appear anywhere else") and it is CORRECT — do not revert it.

   Uses FULL INHERITED membership, exactly as before Assignment 013: belongsToSet with the ancestor
   chain, honouring excludedIds. Every visible item that belongs to the set — directly or through a
   member folder — must be enclosed by the ring.
   This is the accepted Assignment 001-003 behaviour and it must be restored verbatim.

Restore memberRects to the inherited-membership test it used before Assignment 013, while KEEPING the
direct-membership eligibility gate that decides whether the set draws at this level at all.

not share one helper. Name them so the distinction cannot be collapsed again by a future reader.
